### 2026-08-04 — product — T-063/PAS-59 App Icon: **ACCEPT** → `aviran-review`, with the reference-fidelity question carried forward unresolved

- **Did:** acceptance pass on the app icon asset (`passenger-code 4834261`) against `PAS-59`'s four stated requirements. Read workspace-root `CLAUDE.md`, `passenger-code/CLAUDE.md`, this file's designer app-icon entry, both `ios-developer` entries (BLOCKED, then CONFIRMED), the `L-002` founder-direct stub, and `BOARD.md`'s T-063 row before starting. **Looked at the actual asset myself** rather than accepting on the strength of the two upstream reports.
- **Provenance verified, not trusted:** `passenger-code 4834261` and `passenger-brain e1f0714` both confirmed ancestors of `main`. Asset re-confirmed independently: `1024×1024`, 8-bit RGB, non-interlaced, **no alpha** (Apple's hard requirement for the 1024 marketing slot). `Contents.json` read directly — single `universal`/`ios`/`1024x1024` slot carrying `"filename": "AppIcon.png"`, correctly wired.
- **Scope gate (Job 0) run on this inherited task, as required even for founder-direct work:** an app icon is shipping chrome required to submit at all — not a social feature, not onboarding, not Phase 2/3. No strategy-citation issue, nothing to escalate. Noting it was checked rather than skipped because the task arrived without a PRD.

**Verdict: ACCEPT.** Requirement-by-requirement:
1. **Cartoon ice cream truck (P0) — PASS on subject, partial on style.** Subject is unambiguous: rounded cargo box + cab, glass serving window, scalloped pink/green/orange awning, glossy hubcap wheels, soft-serve-cone-on-a-cherry roof ornament. Reads as "ice cream truck" instantly. **Style is the one place I'd push back and am deliberately not rejecting on** — see the reservation below.
2. **Light background matching the reference's light rounded-square treatment (P0) — PASS.** Light-blue vertical gradient with a soft top-left highlight.
3. **Real `AppIcon.appiconset` with `Contents.json` wired — PASS**, verified directly by me above, not carried over from the reports.
4. **Builds and shows on the home screen — PASS.** Accepted `ios-developer`'s build+install+screenshot evidence (`e1f0714`) rather than re-running the full cycle, and independently corroborated the part that doesn't need a build: downscaled the asset to true home-screen and Spotlight sizes (180px = 60pt@3x, 120px = 60pt@2x, 87px = 29pt@3x, Lanczos) and inspected each. Truck silhouette, cone, awning band, green stripe and wheels all still read at 87px. Two independent methods agreeing, not one report echoed.

- **On the three flagged open items — two now closed with measurements, one deliberately left open:**
  1. **No reference image was ever seen by any agent — NOT resolved, and not resolvable by me.** I judged against the text description on its own terms, which is all that exists. My ACCEPT is explicitly bounded: it certifies the icon is a well-executed cartoon ice cream truck on a light background that builds, ships and reads at every real size — **it cannot certify fidelity to an image nobody in this chain has seen.** Only Aviran can close that, and `aviran-review` is exactly the gate for it. Per L-013 I'm also not treating the reference's descriptive specifics ("high-gloss 3D render aesthetic") as founder-given: `chief-of-staff`'s own stub states plainly there is no verified transcript of Aviran's literal wording, so that phrasing is a recorder's description of an unseen image, **[ASSUMPTION]**-grade, and I've declined to reason hard from it.
  2. **True-icon-scale legibility — RESOLVED.** `ios-developer` confirmed in situ at ~230×230px @3x; my own downscale test above agrees down to 87px.
  3. **Full-bleed gradient vs. a baked-in rounded square — RESOLVED, and now measured rather than eyeballed.** Rendered the iOS superellipse mask (n=5) over the asset and counted non-background ink falling outside it: **0.0% clipped** (0 of 54,173 sampled ink pixels). The designer's choice was not merely acceptable, it was the only correct one — Apple *requires* a full-bleed square with no pre-baked corners or alpha for the 1024 marketing icon, so baking the corners in would have been a submission defect.
- **My reservation, recorded but not a REJECT basis — and why I'm not bouncing it.** The brief's described style is a glossy, high-gloss 3D render; what landed is closer to semi-flat vector with soft gradients. Gloss is real in the wheels, cone and window sheen, but the truck body — most of the icon's area — is near-flat white. Related and measurable: **subject and ground are nearly the same brightness** (truck mean luminance 221 vs background mean 227, of 255). In full color that's fine, because the awning and green stripe carry the read. **I am not rejecting on it**, for three reasons taken together: it is taste, not a falsifiable criterion; the criterion it would be judged against traces only to a paraphrase of an unseen image (item 1 above); and bouncing it would send `designer` to iterate against a target no one in the chain can see — the exact shape of the T-062/PAS-58 rejection loop whose loop-guard fired earlier today. One unseeable-reference rejection loop per day is enough. This belongs in front of Aviran as a question, not in front of the designer as a defect.
- **New finding, concrete, not a T-063 blocker — needs its own task with an owner (raising it here per rule 5 rather than leaving it as "flagged for whoever owns it"):** `Contents.json` declares only the single light/universal slot — no `appearances` entries for iOS 18+/26 **dark and tinted** home-screen variants. iOS will auto-generate them. I simulated the tinted variant (grayscale luminance map — **[ASSUMPTION]** an approximation of Apple's actual tinted rendering, not the real algorithm): the truck stays readable, carried by the wheels and hairline outlines, but the awning stripes and green body stripe — all the color work — vanish entirely, leaving a pale, low-contrast tile. Worth an authored dark + tinted pair (`designer` asset, `ios-developer` wiring) as a follow-up. Nothing in `PAS-59`'s request asked for appearance variants, so it is not a basis to withhold acceptance here.
- **Routing:** T-063 → `aviran-review`, per the standing rule that an ACCEPT goes to Aviran's final look before `done`, not straight to `done`. **No PRD `Status:` line to flip** — this task is deliberately PRD-less per Aviran's own routing instruction, so L-006 has nothing to close here; noting that explicitly so the next audit doesn't chase a missing doc.
- **What Aviran should be asked at `aviran-review`, in one line:** does this match the reference icon you had in mind — specifically, is the flatter/softer finish acceptable, or did you want a heavier high-gloss 3D render? Everything else about the icon is verified and ready.
- **Evidence:** `file`/PIL confirmation of 1024×1024 RGB no-alpha; `Contents.json` read in full; 180/120/87px Lanczos downscales inspected visually; superellipse-mask clipping measurement (0 of 54,173 ink px); subject-vs-ground luminance means (221 vs 227); grayscale tinted-variant simulation. No code change made — acceptance is a judgment, not an edit.
- **Left behind:** (1) Aviran's reference-fidelity call is the only thing standing between this and `done`; (2) the dark/tinted appearance-variant follow-up above needs a `BOARD.md` row and an owner — `chief-of-staff`'s to file, since T-063's routing keeps `BOARD.md` and Linear out of my hands this pass; (3) `BOARD.md`/Linear deliberately untouched — `chief-of-staff` reconciles both.
- **Git:** `passenger-brain/agent-os/PROGRESS.md` (this entry only). Explicit path; `git diff --staged --stat` re-read over the **whole index** before committing per rule 2/L-037 — the shared tree carries several other sessions' uncommitted files (`CLAUDE.md`, `agent-os/README.md`, `ONBOARDING.md`, `ACCOUNTS-AND-COSTS.md`, `FOUNDER-WORKFLOW.md`, `agents-mirror/project-manager.md`, a skill file), none staged or touched. Not pushed — Aviran-gated, `CLAUDE.md` rule 9. Hash in the final chat response.

### 2026-08-04 — chief-of-staff — L-002 stub: founder-direct request, disk-space HALT lifted

- **Provenance:** relayed to this session via `main` (the top-level session, not a live chat this session had directly with Aviran) — same relay-of-a-founder-decision shape as this file's earlier "peer development session reported... 4 UI polish fixes" entry, so treated the same way: recorded plainly as a secondhand relay, not dressed up as a captured live quote. **No verbatim Aviran wording is available to me** — `main`'s message paraphrased the content as: Aviran confirmed at the physical machine that disk headroom is healthy, and separately `main` itself re-checked with its own `df -h /` (reported **42Gi avail, 22% used**) as corroboration, explicitly distinguishing this from "another session's re-check" standing in for the human confirmation the `HALT` requires.
- **As I read it:** `main` is reporting that Aviran gave the exact confirmation the `HALT`'s own text names as its lifting condition ("a human at the physical machine frees disk headroom back above ~5GB and says so"), and is instructing: lift the `HALT` and move T-064/PAS-60, T-038/PAS-29, T-062/PAS-58, and T-063/PAS-59 forward. **[ASSUMPTION]** treating this relay as sufficient grounds to act, for three reasons together, not any one alone: (1) `main` is the session this chief-of-staff instance was spawned from and is the only session actually in live chat with Aviran, matching this workspace's own buzz-relay contract for founder-direct requests reaching this agent secondhand; (2) the claim is specific and falsifiable (a human, at the machine, said so) rather than a vague "it's probably fine"; (3) it is independently corroborated, not just asserted — see the next line.

### 2026-08-04 — designer — T-062/PAS-58 round 3: Live Events marker redesigned to a Locali-style diamond+icon

- **Did:** post-ship redesign pass on `EventLayer.swift`'s `LiveEventMarkerGlyph`, picked up from chief-of-staff's dispatch off the founder-direct L-002 stub above ("what is this live event icon? please fix it. review locali assets i like the live events icon there"). Read `passenger-brain/CLAUDE.md`, `passenger-code/CLAUDE.md`, this file's Current Snapshot/recent worklog (the six same-day T-062/PAS-58 entries, both rejection-loop rounds) and `BOARD.md`'s T-062 row first, per protocol — **at the time this session started, `BOARD.md` carried no `HALT` line; one went up mid-task (~19:20, disk 351Mi/98% full) that this session did not re-check for and only found afterward, see the process note below.** Replaced round 2's 18×18 core + two 22×22 legibility-caps-on-stems shape entirely — not tuned — with a single 22×22 rounded square rotated 45° into a diamond, a white `calendar` SF Symbol centered on it, and a pulsing halo (kept round 2's scale+fade `easeOut`/1.6s curve rather than reverting to Locali's exact opacity-only pulse, since nothing in this round's feedback targeted the motion). Modeled directly on the old Locali app's `EventMarker.swift` (`github.com/AviranGrisaro/locali`, pulled by chief-of-staff before dispatch), adapted to Swift 6/this file's structure, not copy-pasted.
- **Occlusion (PRD req 1's legibility bullet) fix, this round:** rather than growing the glyph's own footprint past the Hood label (round 2's approach), nudged the *whole* 44×44 `Annotation` hosting box down 18pt via MapKit's `anchor:` parameter (`UnitPoint(x: 0.5, y: 0.5 − 18/44)`), applied unconditionally (not gated on zoom tier — `EventLayer` has no visibility into `HoodLayer`'s `zoomTier` state, and threading it through for a cosmetic-only difference at non-`.close` zooms was judged more machinery than the nudge warrants; flagged in-code that every marker now sits ~18pt below its true coordinate at zooms where no Hood label is even showing).
- **First attempt, live-measured, reverted:** `.offset(y: 18)` applied to the `Button`. Moved the drawn diamond correctly (pixel measurement below was taken against this build and held), but live tap-testing at the Florentin fixture reproduced **round 2's exact failure mode through a different mechanism** — MapKit's `Annotation` sizes its hit-test box from the content's *un-offset* 44×44 frame, so `.offset()` is rendering-only in this hosting context and the bottom ~14pt of the visibly-shifted diamond fell outside the tappable region. Bottom-of-ink tap opened the **Florentin Hood sheet**, not the event. Caught live (not inferred), fixed by switching to `Annotation`'s own `anchor:` parameter instead, which repositions the whole hosting box (content + hit region together) rather than transforming content inside a fixed box.
- **Live-verified, not code-read-only:** built in an isolated `git worktree` (shared tree has unrelated other-session WIP — `RouteLayer.swift`, `MapScreen.swift`, `events-tel-aviv-seed.json`, etc. — none touched; `git diff --stat` on the worktree showed only `EventLayer.swift` before copying the result into the main tree). Simulator work needed three device cycles — CoreSimulator's booted device disappeared twice mid-session (`xcrun simctl list devices` came back empty each time, a new device had to be created and re-provisioned) — **in hindsight, likely the same disk-pressure condition behind the `HALT` posted mid-task (see process note below), not unrelated flakiness as assumed at the time.** Each retry re-ran `xcrun simctl privacy <udid> grant location-always <bundle-id>` before install and used the existing `-uiTestZoomedIn` seam, temporarily retargeted (never committed, reverted before commit, confirmed via `git diff` showing zero change to `MapScreen.swift`) to the seeded Florentin event's own coordinate (`events-tel-aviv-seed.json` seed-0001, lat 32.057561/lng 34.76928).
  - **Pixel visibility:** 2,914–2,932px of `EventMarker`-colored pixels (light-mode RGB (45,27,105), tol 20) at the true on-centroid coordinate vs. 3,149px for the identical marker rendered clear of any label (event temporarily displaced ~220m north for the reference shot only, reverted before commit, confirmed via `git diff` showing zero change to the seed JSON) — **92.5% visible**, well clear of the PRD's ≥50% floor (`live-events-overlay.md` req 1) and better margin than round 2's 76.4%.
  - **Tap alignment:** tapped both the topmost (y≈447pt) and bottommost (y≈467pt) visible ink pixels at the Florentin fixture. Both now open the event sheet ("Rooftop set, Levinsky", 18:00–22:00) — confirmed only after switching from `.offset()` to `anchor:`; the `.offset()` build failed the bottom tap exactly as described above.
  - **Reduce Motion:** two screenshots 1s apart with Reduce Motion **off** showed a clear pixel diff (halo mid-pulse); the same test with Reduce Motion **on** showed zero diff (`ImageChops.difference(...).getbbox()` → `None`) — halo suppressed, core static, confirmed by contrast rather than by inspection alone.
  - **Build/tests:** `xcodebuild build` succeeded (confirms `Annotation(_:coordinate:anchor:content:)` compiles as used); full `PassengerTests` unit suite — 401 tests across 71 suites, including `EventLayerContrastTests` (2/2) — passed unchanged.
- **Process note — the `HALT` this session should have caught:** `BOARD.md`'s top now carries `> **HALT:** no xcodebuild/simulator work... until a human confirms disk headroom`, posted by `chief-of-staff` at ~19:20 citing 351Mi free/98% full. This session's own device timestamps put its build/simulator work spanning roughly 19:11–19:32 — meaning the back half of this task's simulator work ran *after* the HALT went up, unnoticed, because this session read `BOARD.md` once at dispatch (before the HALT existed) and never re-checked it mid-task, despite hitting repeated unexplained simulator crashes that — with hindsight, and per `designer.md`'s own L-036 instinct to treat an anomaly as a signal rather than push through it — should have prompted a fresh `BOARD.md` read. **Re-checked disk just now (19:37, this session's last action before writing this entry): `df -h /` reads 44Gi free / 22% used** — the low-disk condition, whatever caused it, is no longer present, and every build/test/live-verification result above is real, reproducible evidence, not a guess made under a degraded environment. Flagging the gap in process rather than the result: this session got lucky that the window it worked in wasn't actually starved, not that it correctly honored the HALT protocol. Left the `HALT` banner itself untouched — lifting it needs a human's confirmation per its own text, not a session's own re-check, per the same standard T-038/PAS-29's rounds already established.

- **Spec:** `passenger-brain/design/phase-1/nav-bar-icons-redesign.md`. Concrete deltas per button (no new assets — SwiftUI-only, `ios-developer` implements directly):
  - **Bigger:** frame 44×44 → 52×52pt (+18% linear/+39% area); glyph `.font(.title3)` (~20pt regular) → `.font(.system(size: 24, weight: .semibold))`. 44×44 accessibility floor (Fitts's Law, design-principles.md §2) stays cleared with margin. Five evenly-spread hues, no two adjacent buttons read the same. Contrast against `.thinMaterial` is a reasoned estimate against design-principles.md §5's 3:1 floor, not a measured ratio — flagged for QA to confirm once buildable.
  - **Unique:** kept all 5 existing SF Symbols (no glyph swap, no custom icon set — checked against "would Aviran recognize this as bigger/colored/unique" before considering anything more elaborate) restyled with a colored `Circle().strokeBorder(color, lineWidth: 1.5, opacity 0.9)` ring on top of the unchanged `.thinMaterial` fill, plus the full-opacity colored glyph from above. `.thinMaterial` backdrop kept per requirement #4.
  - **Non-negotiables confirmed untouched:** glyph→button mapping, `isPresented`/`.isSelected` accessibility trait pattern, `accessibilityLabel` text on all 5, and `MapNavRow.swift` itself (row z-order/always-visible/fade behavior) — spec only touches each button's own `.font`/`.foregroundStyle`/`.frame`/`.overlay` styling, verified no existing `HeatButtonInteractionTests`/`ProfileButtonInteractionTests` pin any visual property that would break.

### 2026-08-04 — chief-of-staff — L-002 stub: founder-direct request, nav bar icons

- **Provenance:** founder-direct, live chief-of-staff chat, this session, 2026-08-04. Relayed to this session as a request attributed to Aviran; no independently-verified transcript of his own literal wording exists on my side beyond what this session received, so treating the text below as **the request as relayed to this session**, per L-013's caution against folding restatement into a quotation.
- **Verbatim request:** "i want the nav bar icons will be bigger and with colors, unique."

- **Did:** produced and landed the 1024×1024 App Icon asset for `passenger-code`, per the founder-direct brief (no PRD/TRD gate, per this file's 2026-08-04 "L-002 stub: founder-direct request, app icon" entry, `passenger-brain b00cc0f`). Read workspace-root `CLAUDE.md`, `passenger-brain/CLAUDE.md`, `passenger-code/CLAUDE.md`, this file's Current Snapshot + recent worklog, and `BOARD.md`'s T-063 row first, per standing protocol.

Flagging this plainly rather than implying a viewed reference: nothing here was pattern-matched against an actual image.
- **How the pixels were made:** authored the icon as a layered SVG (gradients for glossy shading, no flat-vector look) — a white/pale-blue cartoon ice cream truck (rounded cargo box + cab, two wheels with glossy hubcap highlights, a glass serving window, a scalloped orange/pink/green awning that echoes the reference's stripe palette, and a soft-serve-cone-on-a-cherry roof ornament for silhouette recognition at a glance) on a full-bleed light-blue gradient background with a soft top-left highlight for extra gloss. First pass left too much empty background above/below the truck; rescaled+recentered the truck group (`scale(1.15)` about its own bounding-box center) to fill the frame more evenly (93% width / ~70% height, symmetric margins) before finalizing. Rasterized with `rsvg-convert` (available via Homebrew) at exact `-w 1024 -h 1024`, then verified and re-saved as flat RGB (no alpha) via Pillow — confirmed `1024×1024`, `8-bit/color RGB, non-interlaced`, `has alpha: False` before it left the scratchpad. Sanity-checked legibility by downscaling to 120×120 and 60×60 with Lanczos resampling and visually inspecting both: the truck silhouette + cone + stripe band still read clearly as "ice cream truck" at 60×60, though the fine interior details (mirror, door handle, headlight) disappear as expected at that size — flagging that as an open item for product's acceptance pass, not resolving it myself.
- **Landed:** `passenger-code/Passenger/Assets.xcassets/AppIcon.appiconset/AppIcon.png` (new file, 1024×1024 PNG, RGB, no alpha, 184447 bytes) and `passenger-code/Passenger/Assets.xcassets/AppIcon.appiconset/Contents.json` (added `"filename" : "AppIcon.png"` to the single existing `universal`/`ios`/`1024x1024` entry — that slot previously had no `filename` key at all, confirmed empty before starting, per the dispatch brief). Did not run a full Xcode build/verify pass myself — that's `ios-developer`'s follow-up per the brief — but the asset + `Contents.json` wiring are both committed so that step is a straight verify, not a redo.
- **Open items for product's acceptance pass (flagged, not silently resolved):** (1) no actual reference image was ever seen by any agent in this chain — verify the result actually matches what Aviran had in mind, since it was built from a text description alone; (2) "ice cream truck" legibility at true iOS home-screen scale (much smaller than my 60×60 desktop-resolution test, and rendered through iOS's own corner mask/shadow) is untested — worth a real-device/simulator look once `ios-developer` wires it in; (3) I deviated from the reference's exact "rounded-square background" description by using a plain gradient rect per rule 3's "no pre-baked corner rounding" requirement — iOS applies its own mask at render time, so the reference's rounded-square look should already appear once installed, but flagging the reasoning in case that's not what was meant.
- **Git:** `passenger-code 4834261` (explicit paths — only `AppIcon.png` + `Contents.json` staged; re-checked `git diff --staged --stat` before commit, shared tree currently carries substantial unrelated uncommitted work from other sessions — Scenic Walk routing, `EventDetailRows.swift`, `HoodLayer.swift`, etc. — none of it touched or swept in). `passenger-brain` (this entry) — hash in the final chat response. - **Req 4 bullet 1 — FAILS, live-demonstrated, and it is new to this fix.** The hit region did not grow with the drawn mark: `EventLayer.swift:28`'s `Button` frame is still 44×44 and `MapScreen.swift:845`'s `handleTap` still uses `mapPointTolerance(forScreenPoints: 22)`, i.e. a 22pt radius — while the caps now sit 19–41pt from center. Roughly 9% of each cap's area is inside the tap circle. **Tapped the top cap at (201, 407) pt: the Florentin *Hood* sheet opened, not the event.** Tapped the center at (201, 437) pt — where the marker core is 0px visible under the name pill — and the event sheet ("Rooftop set, Levinsky") opened correctly. So at the exact coordinate this fix exists to serve, **100% of the ink a user can see is dead and falls through to `HoodLayer`, and the only working tap point is the one that looks like a Hood label.** The perceived affordance and the real one are now inverted.
- **The footprint/silhouette question `chief-of-staff` flagged — I agree with the concern, and it is a REJECT basis on its own.** Aviran: "the icon of the live events is too big… more solid and small." Measured 22 × 82 pt against the original 44 × 44. Total *ink area* did fall (~1,372 pt² vs 1,936 pt²), which is what the file's doc comment argues — but perceived size tracks extent and articulation, and both moved the wrong way. (a) The vertical extent nearly doubled past the size that drew the complaint. (b) "Solid" is satisfied at the pixel level and lost at the gestalt level: one filled mark became three stacked squares on a stalk — it reads as a barbell/chain-link, not a point marker, and nothing in it indicates *which* point on the map the event is at. (c) At `.close` zoom the "Florentin" pill sits *between* the two caps, so the marker reads as brackets decorating the Hood label rather than as its own object. (d) **At cold-open city-wide zoom, where no Hood label is drawn at all, the caps have no function and the marker is the single most visually dominant object on screen** — the "too big" complaint returns in a worse form at the zoom where this fix's own justification does not apply. Captured live; not a taste call made from the diff.
- **Not re-derived, confirmed unaffected by this diff:** 44×44 `Button` frame, `accessibilityLabel`, `accessibilityIdentifier` byte-identical (read); contrast unchanged (`#2D1B69` untouched, so round 1's ~11.7:1 stands); Reduce Motion gating logic untouched — only the halo's peak-opacity constant changed, and the caps are correctly *ungated* since they are shape, not motion. Halo observed rendering live mid-cycle at the new 0.65 peak; more noticeable than round 1, no longer a concern.
- **Loop-guard: this is the 2nd bounce on one requirement** (build → REJECT → fix → REJECT), and both rejections trace to the same underlying tension — a small marker and `HoodLayer`'s centroid label competing for the same pixels. Per `CLAUDE.md`'s loop-guard, **escalating to Aviran rather than auto-redispatching `designer` a third time.** The lever nobody has pulled is on the *label* side; the designer tested only `MapContent` declaration order, which is not the same as a z-order or collision fix. Options for Aviran to pick between, none decided by me: **(1)** move the marker off the centroid — `Annotation(anchor:)` or a small coordinate nudge — so one compact 18–24pt mark sits clear of the label band, keeping one gestalt and letting the tap region travel with it; **(2)** suppress whichever of the two collides (event marker vs. Hood name pill) when they coincide — the same unowned annotation-collision gap flagged at round 1 and adjacent to T-061; **(3)** accept a smaller mark with an honestly lower legibility number, if Aviran prefers "small" to "strictly ≥50%". **Whatever ships, the drawn mark must fit inside the hit region** — either the mark shrinks to ≤44pt, or the `Button` frame *and* `handleTap`'s 22pt tolerance grow with it. That mismatch is what turned this round's visible ink into dead pixels.
- **PRD fixed in the same commit (L-009):** `prds/live-events-overlay/live-events-overlay.md` req 4 gains a tappable-ink bullet ("tap the topmost and bottommost pixel of the marker's drawn ink; both open the event sheet"), plus a Decisions-log row. Round 1's legibility bullet floored what a user can *see* and said nothing about whether that ink *does* anything — the fix satisfied it by growing the mark past the target. Stated as which sheet opens, not as a geometry constraint, so a future fix may shrink the mark or grow the target. HTML twin regenerated via `md2html.convert()` on that one file only (rule 2). PRD `Status:` line untouched — this is a REJECT, nothing to flip.
- **Side-finding, routed not absorbed:** the `simctl privacy … grant location-always` pre-grant is **not** durable, confirming `qa`'s round-12 finding over round 1's more optimistic reading — on a device with prior app state the dialog reappeared within 3 s. It *is* reliable after `simctl erase` + boot + install + grant + launch, which is the recipe that worked here and is worth handing to `qa` for T-038/PAS-29 verbatim rather than the shorter version.
- **Also seen, not mine and already owned:** the event sheet renders `florentin` and `music` raw (req 4 bullet 4). That fix is another session's uncommitted in-flight work on `Passenger/Events/EventDetailRows.swift` (`displayCategory`, T-052/PAS-40) — not on `main` at `1036a00`, not a T-062 regression, no action taken.
- **Evidence:** `xcodebuild build` → succeeded (3×: base, Florentin-camera, displaced-seed reference). Screenshots + crops in this session's scratchpad (`occluded_final.png`, `reference.png`, `tap_topcap.png`, `tap_center.png`, `coldopen.png`) — session-local, not committed. Worktree removed; `passenger-code` shared tree's modified/untracked set identical before and after, `HEAD` still `1036a00`.

### 2026-08-04 — product — T-062/PAS-58 Live Events marker redesign, acceptance: **REJECT** → `build`(designer)

- **Did:** acceptance pass on `designer`'s post-ship marker redesign (`passenger-code c2770541`). Read `passenger-brain/CLAUDE.md`, this file's Current Snapshot/recent worklog, and `BOARD.md`'s T-062 row first. **Got a live visual render — this verdict is not a code-read.** The designer's and `qa`'s simulator interaction block did not reproduce: pre-granting location with `xcrun simctl privacy <udid> grant location-always` **before** first launch means the permission dialog never appears, so the map is reachable without any tap. Worked in a detached `git worktree` at `c277054` with a dedicated simulator and derived-data path; the shared tree was never touched (`EventLayer.swift` clean throughout).
- **Verdict: REJECT.** Requirements 1, 3, 4 and P1 pass. Requirement 2 fails on its purpose clause — the founder asked for a marker "that will let the user know that there is a live event," and the shipped marker can render **completely invisible**.
- **The finding, measured, same camera/zoom/simulator/build-config, marker design the only variable:**

  | build | event fixture position | marker pixels on screen | visible size |
  |---|---|---|---|
  | old marker (`3435d85`) | seeded, at the Florentin Hood centroid | 8,812 | 43.0 × 43.7 pt |
  | **new marker (`c277054`)** | **seeded, same coordinate** | **0** | **not visible at all** |
  | new marker (`c277054`) | shifted ~190 m off the centroid | 2,545 | 17.7 × 17.7 pt |

  Cause: `HoodLayer.swift:96`'s centroid `Annotation` (the "Florentin" name pill, ~16–20pt tall, drawn above `EventLayer`'s annotation) fully covers the new 18pt core. The old 44pt mark extended past the pill in every direction and could not be hidden this way. **Measured:** it fires at `.close` zoom on the exact seeded fixture the team demos with, whenever an event's coordinate falls inside the label pill's footprint. **[ASSUMPTION]** — how often real Tel Aviv events land that close to a Hood centroid is unmeasured; I did not size it, and it should not be reported as if I had. What is not an assumption is that the failure mode exists, is reproducible, and is new to this redesign. Cold-open city-wide zoom is unaffected — Hood labels don't render there, and the marker read cleanly.
- **Verified live, passing — not accepted on code-read:** (a) **micro-animation runs.** `simctl io screenshot` returns a settled frame and showed 8 byte-identical captures, which is a capture artifact, not a dead animation — video at 10fps shows the halo band's mean luminance cycling with minima 16 frames apart, i.e. **1.6 s**, matching the spec exactly. (b) **Reduce Motion is genuinely honoured.** With `ReduceMotionEnabled` set and the device rebooted, the same band is flat across 32 frames (spread 0.7/255, against 6.9 with motion on) and the core still renders at 18pt. (c) Core measures 17.7 × 17.7 pt, contrast ≈ **11.7:1** against the map's light tiles — well past WCAG AA's 3:1 for UI components (`design/design-principles.md` §4). **Size and contrast are not the defect; occlusion is.** (d) Tap target, `accessibilityLabel`, `accessibilityIdentifier` unchanged — confirmed by read, matching `chief-of-staff`'s independent check.
- **Second finding, designer's judgment to weigh, not a hard fail:** the pulse is at the low end of noticeable. Its mean luminance swing over the halo band is ~7/255 (≈2.7%) across the full cycle; peak per-pixel delta between cycle extremes is 164/765. It reads as a soft breath rather than a "live" cue, and it does nothing at all when the core is occluded. The halo is the natural place to fix both — worth revisiting amplitude/extent in the same pass.
- **Explicitly not asking for a revert to 44pt.** Aviran's "too big" stands; smaller and more solid is the right direction and requirement 1 is met. What's missing is a legibility floor. Options are the designer's call: raise `EventLayer` above the centroid annotation in the `MapContent` z-order, give the core a light outline/ring that extends past the label pill, or bump the core modestly (~24–26pt, still far under 44).
- **PRD fixed in this same commit (L-009):** `prds/live-events-overlay/live-events-overlay.md` req 1 gains a marker-legibility bullet stating the rendered consequence ("at least half the marker's drawn area still shows"), plus a Decisions-log row. Every earlier gate passed this redesign because no bullet ever required the marker to be *visible* — "distinguishable from place pins" presumes it renders. ### 2026-08-04 — designer — T-062/PAS-58: Live Events marker redesign — smaller, solid, micro-pulse

- **Did:** post-ship redesign pass against the founder-direct request recorded at this file's 2026-08-04 L-002 stub ("the icon of the live events is too big. we need something more solid and small with micro animation that will let the user know that there is a live event"). Read `passenger-brain/CLAUDE.md`, `passenger-code/CLAUDE.md`, this file's Current Snapshot/recent worklog, and `BOARD.md`'s T-062 row first, per protocol. Worked against the actual shipped code (`passenger-code/Passenger/Map/EventLayer.swift`), not a fresh mockup — no mockup produced or needed, per the retired pre-build gate not applying here.
- **Change:** replaced the 44×44 fully-filled `RoundedRectangle` + thin `Image(systemName: "sparkles")` glyph with a new `LiveEventMarkerGlyph`: an 18×18 solid rounded-square core plus a 30×30 halo that pulses outward and fades, looping via `.repeatForever`. Halo is capped well inside the marker's own 44×44 tap footprint, so it can't compete with the heatmap's own area/fill channel — the exact guarantee `EventLayer.swift`'s own doc comment already made (§2.3 req 2 bullet 1), unchanged by this pass.
- **Requirement-by-requirement:**
  1. Smaller/more solid — visible mark shrank from filling the full 44pt frame to an 18pt solid core (no thin glyph on top).
  2. Micro-animation communicating "live" without competing with heat's area channel — the pulsing halo, capped at 30pt, well under any heat-polygon scale.
  3. Reduce Motion — `@Environment(\.accessibilityReduceMotion)` (SwiftUI-native, per the task's own preferred form). When on, the halo view isn't even constructed and no animation starts; core renders solid and static.
  4. Tap target/accessibility — 44×44 `Button` frame, `accessibilityLabel("\(event.name), event, \(event.timeLabel)")`, and `accessibilityIdentifier("eventMarker-\(event.id)")` are all byte-for-byte unchanged; only the drawn content inside the frame changed.
  5. P1 shape reconsideration — kept the rounded-square silhouette (not a diamond/star) rather than `PlaceLayer`'s `Circle`: at 18pt map scale a diamond reads close enough to a small circle to blur the exact place/event greyscale distinction the shape exists for (`design-principles.md` §3, "never rely on color alone"); rounded square stays unambiguous at the smaller size. Considered, documented in the file's own doc comment, not applied.
- **Build verification, honestly reported:** the shared `passenger-code` working tree is heavily dirty with several other sessions' in-flight, non-compiling WIP (a `RoutePreview.swift:30` "`ScenicUnavailable` does not conform to `Error`" failure unrelated to this change, confirmed by building the shared tree first and seeing the failure land elsewhere, not in `EventLayer.swift`). To get an honest signal isolated from that, built in a detached `git worktree` pinned to `HEAD` (`3435d85`) with only this file's diff applied, dedicated `-derivedDataPath`: **BUILD SUCCEEDED**, no warnings on the new code. Installed and launched on a booted `iPhone 17 Pro` simulator; app opens to the map. **Could not get a live on-device screenshot of the marker itself** — the same location-permission-dialog interaction block `qa`'s T-038/PAS-29 round-10/11 passes already hit and documented in this file (taps/pinch gestures on the map not registering, even directly on map content, not just chrome buttons) reproduced here too; not re-investigated, since it's already an open, flagged, pre-existing environment issue, not something this pass caused or could fix. Cleaned up: uninstalled the test build, removed the worktree and its dedicated derived data (disk recovered 4.7Gi → 8.1Gi free).
- **Left behind / open for `product`'s acceptance pass:** (1) no live visual render was captured — acceptance will need either a working simulator interaction path or a device to actually see the pulse; (2) the halo's exact timing (1.6s ease-out loop) and opacity/scale curve (0.45→0, 0.6×→1×) are a first-pass judgment call, not measured against any prior art — flag if it reads too fast/slow/subtle once seen live; (3) the "solid, no icon" choice (dropping `sparkles` entirely rather than swapping to a bolder glyph) rests on `EventLayer.swift`'s own pre-existing reasoning that shape, not the icon, carries the place/event distinction — worth a second look from `product`/Aviran if a fully iconless marker reads as less obviously "an event" than intended.
- **Git:** `passenger-code c2770541` (`Passenger/Map/EventLayer.swift`, explicit path staged). `passenger-brain/agent-os/BOARD.md` (T-062 row updated), `passenger-brain/agent-os/PROGRESS.md` (this entry). Not pushed — Aviran-gated, `CLAUDE.md` rule 9. ### 2026-08-04 — designer — T-060/PAS-56: PAS-49's border-only `HoodLayer` render — **intended, not a regression**; `map-rendering-spec.md` rewritten; T-061 filed for a real gap it surfaced

- **Did:** resolved the question `architect` filed at T-060/PAS-56 while amending `prds/search-quick-filters/TRD.md` to v3 — was `PAS-49`'s rewrite of `HoodLayer.body` (dropping the interior fill, moving heat's color into the outline stroke) intended, or an undocumented regression that `TRD.md` v3's `borderColor` retarget (§9 row 4, step C15) was wrong to assume? Read `passenger-brain/CLAUDE.md`, this file's Current Snapshot/recent worklog, and `BOARD.md`'s T-060 row first, then went to source rather than the worklog's paraphrase: `passenger-code` commits `c1b8bc3`/`5e1f72f` (`git show`, full messages), `HoodLayer.swift` in full, and `design/map-rendering-spec.md` in full.
- **Conclusion: intended.** `HoodLayer.swift:157-164`'s doc comment states it directly — "Design fix (2026-08-04): a Hood is marked by its border alone, never by a filled interior… The color that used to live in the interior fill now lives in this line instead" — and reasons through the specific opacity boost (past `fillColor`'s 0.16/0.38/0.62 steps) needed to keep a thin stroke legible, which is not something an accidental change acquires. `c1b8bc3`'s commit message frames it as one of "Four design fixes (PAS-47, PAS-48, PAS-49, PAS-50)," not a bugfix or refactor. Per this file's own 2026-08-04 entry ("4 UI polish tickets filed… founder-direct"), the four tickets were relayed as a direct Aviran request to the building session — secondhand, no verbatim quote captured, so not treated as proof on its own — but the code-level evidence stands independent of that provenance and doesn't read as oversight either way.
- **Spec rewritten, not the code:** `design/map-rendering-spec.md` §2's zoom table and surrounding note, and §3's opening bullet, now describe the border-only reality — heat and the tourist-trap flag share one visual channel (the outline stroke) but never the same line at the same time: `HoodLayer.body`'s `effectiveStroke` switch is mutually exclusive per Hood, so a flagged zone's stroke is always `Color("Flag")` at a distinct weight/dash and never blends with `borderColor`'s heat hue. "Never rely on color alone" (design-principles.md §3) still holds on that basis.
- **Real gap surfaced, not silently absorbed: a flagged zone now shows zero heat-intensity signal on the map.** Before `PAS-49`, fill carried heat regardless of flag state; after, a flagged zone's entire outline is devoted to the flag's color, so heat gradation for tourist-trap zones is only recoverable by tapping in. This is a genuine loss against the product's "how busy right now, one glance" north star, specifically for the zones the busy+flagged warning exists to call out. **Filed as `BOARD.md` T-061**, `designer`/`product`, backlog, non-blocking — three options sketched there (low-opacity heat wash behind the flag outline, a heat cue folded into the centroid label, or documenting the trade-off and closing as won't-fix), none decided.
- **Consequence for `PAS-55`: none — the TRD v3 amendment and build step C15 stand as written.** They were built on the [ASSUMPTION] that `PAS-49` was intended; that's now confirmed, so there's nothing to revert. `HoodLayer.fillColor`'s eventual deletion (blocked on this ticket per T-060's original note) is now separately unblocked, though still contingent on C15 landing first.
- **Left behind:** T-061 needs a design decision (or an explicit won't-fix) from whoever picks it up — not urgent, doesn't block C15, `qa`'s row 4(a) live check, or anything else on `PAS-55`'s chain. Not yet a Linear issue for either T-060's resolution or T-061 — `chief-of-staff` to ticket if it wants one (T-060 as a closed doc/verdict note, T-061 as a new backlog item).
- **Git:** `passenger-brain/design/map-rendering-spec.md`, `passenger-brain/agent-os/BOARD.md` (T-060 resolved, T-061 added), `passenger-brain/agent-os/PROGRESS.md` (this entry). Explicit paths, not pushed — Aviran-gated, `CLAUDE.md` rule 9. Hash in the final chat response. No `passenger-code` changes made by this session.

### 2026-08-04 — architect — T-057/PAS-46 Scenic Walk TRD: amendment A3 (runtime camera inset, RouteControls accessibility row)

- **Did:** amended `prds/scenic-walk/TRD.md` in place to resolve `ios-code-reviewer`'s findings **2 and 3** from its trd-review AGREE (`passenger-brain dd68110`) — the two its own review, and my previous T-057 pass, both named as deserving the same doc-only treatment A1/A2 got. Documentation-only, no re-review cycle owed. Read `passenger-brain/CLAUDE.md`, this file's recent worklog, and the TRD in full first; checked `passenger-code/Passenger/Map/ProfileButton.swift` and `SearchButton.swift` directly rather than taking the review note's word for the "every other interactive control" claim — both are literally `.frame(width: 44, height: 44)` with the comment *"Fitts's Law minimum (design-principles.md §2)"* plus an `.accessibilityLabel`, so §9 row 15 is written against the real idiom.
- **A3(a) — the camera bottom inset is measured, never hardcoded.** §4.9 justified the inset with `qa`'s single iPhone 17 measurement (`.medium` sheet at y 415–866). That measurement is the **evidence the inset is needed** and is the wrong thing to ship: a `.medium` detent's height moves with Dynamic Type and with iPad/landscape size classes, and a route fitted against a stale constant lands under the sheet in exactly the configurations least likely to be reviewed. §4.9 now requires the inset to be read from the sheet's **rendered** frame at every fit, names the mechanism in preference order (height published up from the sheet content's `onGeometryChange`/`GeometryReader` to `MapScreen`, which already owns both surfaces — no new plumbing layer; else a `safeAreaInset`-based fit), and states two hard rules: derived from a measured frame every time, and **no device-specific pixel constant anywhere in the fit path**. One stated edge: a resolve completing before first layout fits with **zero** inset and re-fits once on the first height report — the only re-fit this feature permits, and explicitly not a second camera move in the sense §9 row 12 forbids.
- **A3(b) — §9 gained row 15, and row 12 gained the check that actually catches a constant.** Row 12 now runs **twice** — default size and `UICTContentSizeCategoryAccessibilityExtraExtraExtraLarge` — asserting first that the sheet is genuinely taller at the larger size, then that the containment still holds **and the two runs' camera regions are not identical**, which is precisely what a hardcoded inset would produce; plus a grep that fails on a device constant in the fit path, with a positive control. **Row 15** (new, per L-032 — a normative statement with no row is a rule no gate reads) covers `RouteControls`: element count **3** asserted before any ∀, then 44×44 frames; VoiceOver labels that carry duration/distance and, in each of the 6 disabled cases, the §4.10 **reason** — so a VoiceOver user gets what a sighted user reads off the visible line, with `isEnabled == false` asserted alongside rather than instead; and AX5 with no truncation of the duration/distance/`via <Hood>` text, checked on rendered geometry. **Row 15 deliberately does not grep `.lineLimit`** — T-038 §9 row 8(c-ii) already established that `.lineLimit`'s absence is not what prevents truncation; `.fixedSize(horizontal: false, vertical: true)` is, and its presence is a supporting fact, not the check.
- **Sections touched:** §4.9, §9 rows 12 and 15 (new), §10 C5/C6/C7/C8, header amendment table (new **A3** row), §12, new decision **D17**.
- **Two of `ios-code-reviewer`'s five findings deliberately left open, and now named in §12 rather than left in a worklog entry:** its finding 1 (the 2.5 s `resolveBudget` is enforced between candidate attempts, not within one, so a single slow-but-not-throttled leg has no forced cutoff) and finding 4 (the camera fit animation doesn't address Reduce Motion). Both are **behaviour** changes, not verification defects, so folding them into a documentation-only amendment would have been the wrong instrument — they stay `ios-developer`'s build-time calls, but they are now written into the document instead of living only in a review comment.
- **Evidence:** no code written or run — design-document amendment. Table integrity re-checked after editing (a blank line had split the §9 table when row 15 was inserted; found and fixed before commit). Claims about the existing app verified against source this session, not from the review note.
- **Left behind:** C5/C6/C7/C8 now carry A3's requirements inline, so nothing here needs to be remembered separately by whoever builds them. §12's items 2–6 stand unchanged, including D9's mini-map reading (still wants `product`/Aviran) and §8 R1 (Aviran's).

### 2026-08-04 — architect — T-038/PAS-55 search-quick-filters TRD §9 v3: the dim row retargeted from `fillColor` to `borderColor`

- **Did:** amended `prds/search-quick-filters/TRD.md` to v3, resolving `qa`'s **F-QA5** (raised in the T-038/PAS-29 BLOCKED pass, `passenger-brain a562be4`, filed as `PAS-55`). Read `passenger-brain/CLAUDE.md`, this file's recent worklog including the full `qa` entry, and the TRD in full; then read `passenger-code/Passenger/Map/HoodLayer.swift` directly rather than working from the QA note.
- **The defect, confirmed at source:** `PAS-49` (`passenger-code c1b8bc3`/`5e1f72f`) rewrote `HoodLayer.body` to draw `.foregroundStyle(.clear)` in every stroke branch. `fillColor` — the property §9 row 4 named and the only thing `HoodLayerFillDimTests` asserts — is no longer read by `body`. It still computes correctly, so the suite stays green and proves nothing. `borderColor` (`HoodLayer.swift:165-169`) is the channel that renders and does carry `* dimOpacity` in both branches, with **zero** test coverage anywhere.
- **Amended:** §9 row 4 **(a)/(b)/(f)** retargeted at `borderColor`; §4.10 rewritten to describe the border-only render; new build step **C15**; new decision **D15**; D14's now-superseded 4(a) row annotated in place rather than rewritten; a **third standing rule** added to §9's preamble.
- **Four calls inside the amendment that were not in the QA finding, stated rather than buried.** (1) **Row 4(a) asserts the 0.25 *ratio*, not just "strictly less"** — an inequality passes against a floored or clamped multiplier; the ratio doesn't. (2) **Three fixture conditions that silently invalidate the run if unmet:** `isHovered == false` on all 24 layers (`hoverGlow`'s 1.4 is a second uncontrolled input to the same alpha — it did not exist when v2 was written), at least one Hood per band (`borderColor` clamps at `min(bandStrength × hoverGlow, 1)`, and a single-band fixture cannot tell a correct multiply from a clamped one), and the coverage grep run **first**. (3) **Row 4(f) was wrong in a way the retarget exposes, not created:** a `nil`-band Hood renders a visible neutral `.secondary` hairline, so a check expecting "nothing renders" would **fail a correct implementation**; and under the old fill wording, "every one of the other 23 dims strictly" was actually *false* for any `nil`-band Hood among the 23, since `.clear` dims to `.clear`. The border channel fixes both. (4) **`UIColor(Color.secondary.opacity(x))` is named as the one known fragility** — if it doesn't resolve to a comparable alpha in a unit test, the `nil`-band Hoods are recorded as **BLOCKED or checked rendered**, never passed by omission.
- **§4.10's `nil`-band limit is narrowed, not resolved — flagged to `product`.** Under border-only rendering a matched `nil`-band Hood keeps a **full-strength neutral outline** while the other 23 drop to 0.25, so there is now a positive mark on the match rather than only the absence of dimming elsewhere. The surviving limit is that the mark is **uncoloured**, not that it is absent. `product` should confirm that reading rather than inherit v2's wording.
- **Why `fillColor` is not deleted, since it is the obvious fix:** deletion is a production change to a shipped file, on a task sitting at `qa`, to fix a *verification* defect — the two shouldn't be bundled, and the design question below should settle first. Named in D15 rather than quietly skipped. **C15 changes no production behaviour**: it makes `borderColor` internal (the same trade `fillColor` already made and documented) and rewrites the suite.
- **The general lesson, written into §9 as a standing rule.** This is L-009's **third** occurrence on this one requirement (T-046/LOC-104, T-053/PAS-43, now this) and the **second on row 4**. The first two were wrong-*layer* checks. This one is different and worse: the check was written at the right layer and **became** wrong when the layer moved underneath it. So the new rule is not "check the render" — v2 already said that — it is: **name the channel the view draws, and anchor every colour/opacity row on a positive coverage grep, so losing that channel's tests fails the row instead of silently passing it.** A verification row is a claim about the code and it decays.
- **Drift found, not mine to fix, and it has no row yet:** `design/map-rendering-spec.md` §2 still states *"heat is always the area/fill treatment"* — flatly contradicted by the shipped border-only render — and `PAS-49`'s change is recorded in **no** PRD, TRD or design doc anywhere in `passenger-brain`. **Filed as `BOARD.md` row T-060**, owner `designer` (that file's owner), `product` if it turns out to be scope rather than documentation — a row, not a worklog line, per rule 5 / L-026. **Worth recording how close that came to not happening:** when I started, `BOARD.md` carried another session's uncommitted edits and rule 2 would have blocked me from staging it — leaving rule 5's own remedy unavailable, since the file I was told to file the finding *on* was the blocked one. Two commits (`df88b91`, `b859f56`) landed mid-session and cleaned it, so I re-checked `git status` at commit time instead of trusting the reading from the start of my turn, and the row exists. If `BOARD.md` had still been dirty this would have been a worklog line and nothing more, which is exactly the failure L-026 describes. **[ASSUMPTION]**, stated in D15 and in T-060: `PAS-49` was an intended design fix, not a regression — its commit message reads as deliberate. If T-060 concludes otherwise, §9 row 4 and C15 revert with it.
- **Evidence:** no code written or run — design-document amendment. `HoodLayer.swift` read in full (the `body` branches, `fillColor`'s doc comment, `borderColor`, `hoverGlow`, `strokeWidth`). `grep` over `design/`, `prds/` and `strategy/decisions.md` for any record of the border-only decision → **0 hits**, which is the basis of the drift finding above. Table integrity re-checked after editing.
- **Left behind:** (1) **C15 is `ios-developer`'s** — retarget `HoodLayerFillDimTests` at `borderColor` per §9 row 4, make `borderColor` internal, keep the `fillColor` assertions as a cheap guard; the suite name is now false advertising and can be changed in the same pass. (2) `qa`'s row 4(a) live before/after comparison is still owed once the machine has headroom, and should now be run against **borders**, not fills. (3) **T-060** is on the board for `designer` — the border-only render is undocumented and contradicts `map-rendering-spec.md` §2; `chief-of-staff` to ticket it in Linear if it wants one. (4) `fillColor`'s eventual deletion, once T-060 settles.

### 2026-08-04 — architect — T-057/PAS-46 Scenic Walk TRD: two doc-only pre-C3 amendments (Sendable safety, memo lifetime)

- **Did:** amended `prds/scenic-walk/TRD.md` in place to resolve the two pre-C3 findings from `ios-developer`'s trd-review AGREE (`passenger-brain 8a006f7`). Documentation-only, no re-review cycle owed — same treatment as the T-036/T-034 precedent, and the treatment `ios-developer` itself recommended. Read `passenger-brain/CLAUDE.md`, this file's recent worklog (all four T-057 sign-offs: `8a006f7`, `dd68110`, `5bfee4d`, and `code-reviewer`'s A1 half), `BOARD.md`'s T-057 row, and the full TRD before editing. Also read `passenger-code/Passenger/Map/MapScreen.swift`, `Places/SavedPlacesStore.swift` and `PassengerApp.swift` directly rather than taking the review note's word for the store-ownership pattern — the app has no app-level store layer at all; `MapScreen` owns every store as `@State` and injects them with `.environment(...)` on one specific `Group`, whose own comment documents why a sheet's content closure doesn't inherit environment values applied elsewhere. A2's owner is named against that real call site, not against an invented singleton.
- **Amendment A1 — `WalkingRouteProvider` no longer passes `MKMapItem`.** The protocol now takes a new `RouteEndpoint` value type (`.currentLocation` | `.coordinate(_, name:)`), and every MapKit object is constructed, used and discarded inside one async function body in the `MKDirections` conformer (§4.1 now shows that conformer, including the private `mapItem(for:)` helper). `MKMapItem` is not `Sendable`, and the original signature crossed both the `@MainActor`→provider hop and §4.6's concurrent two-leg fan-out; at `SWIFT_STRICT_CONCURRENCY = complete` that is a build failure or an `@unchecked Sendable`, and `passenger-code/CLAUDE.md` bans the latter — so "make it compile" would have meant breaking a standing engineering rule at C3, under build pressure, by whoever hit it first. **This strengthens D3's privacy contract rather than touching it:** the origin is now a case with no payload, so there is nowhere in the design a user coordinate could be stored even by accident. Sections touched: §2.1, §2.2, §3.2, §3.3, §4.1, §4.2, §4.3, §4.6, §10 C1/C3, new decision **D15**.
- **Amendment A2 — the 5-minute memo moved to `RouteMemoStore`.** §4.4 said `RoutePreviewModel` is torn down per presentation while also holding a memo whose entire purpose is surviving re-presentation; §4.7 built the ≤3-requests-per-modal-open promise on that memo. Both could not be true. The memo now lives in `RouteMemoStore` — one instance, `@State` on `MapScreen`, injected on the same `Group` as `placeCatalog`/`detailRouter`/`savedPlacesStore`, cleared from the existing `.onChange(of: scenePhase)` on `.background`, TTL evaluated on read so there is no timer. `RoutePreviewModel` keeps its per-presentation lifetime and now holds **no** cross-place state at all. Sections touched: §2.1, §2.2, §3.3, §4.4, §4.7, §5, §10 C1/C3/C6, **D11** amended, new decision **D16**.
- **Three calls made inside the amendments that were not in the review note, stated rather than buried:** (1) **only terminal `.resolved` states are memoised** — `.failed`, `.noOrigin`, `.resolving` and `.idle` are not, so a transient failure or a permission state retries on the next open instead of being cached for five minutes; a stated-failure `.resolved` (e.g. `.noQualifyingHood`) *is* memoised, being as expensive to recompute and as stable as a success. (2) `reset()` clears the presentation's geometry but never the memo — a dismissal is not an invalidation. (3) §3.2 now names two conformance facts C1 would otherwise hit mid-file: `CLLocationCoordinate2D` is **not** `Equatable` in the SDK, so `RoutePlan`'s and `RouteEndpoint`'s `Equatable` conformances need hand-written `==` (true of the original draft too — written down now because A1 added a second type of the same shape), and its `Sendable`-ness comes from being an imported C struct, with a named two-`Double` fallback if the iOS 26 SDK disagrees. **[ASSUMPTION]** on all three, all cheap to reverse, none changing a requirement or a bound.
- **§9 grew from 12 rows to 14, per L-032** — every normative statement gets a row, and both amendments introduce normative statements. **Row 13** (A1): the protocol signature contains `RouteEndpoint` twice and `MKMapItem` zero times; MapKit reference types appear in exactly one file under `Passenger/Routing`; `@unchecked Sendable`/`nonisolated(unsafe)` → 0 hits with a positive control; and the target builds with **0 concurrency warnings** in `Passenger/Routing` at `complete` — a warning there is the defect A1 exists to prevent, so it is a failure, not noise. **Row 14** (A2): resolve on model **A**, discard it, resolve the same place on a new model **B** against the same store → +0 provider calls, **with a fresh-store control run asserting 3** so the row cannot pass against a provider that is simply never called; plus expiry asserted on both sides of the TTL boundary with an injected `now`, `clearAll()` swept over an id count asserted ≥ 2 first, and cross-place isolation. **Row 10 was the load-bearing edit:** its "+0 on re-open" pass condition now *requires the test to destroy the `RoutePreviewModel` between opens* — reusing a live model would have made that row pass with no memo existing at all, which is exactly the defect A2 fixed. Row 11(d) retargeted at `RouteMemoStore` with a positive control, and 11(a) gained a note that the conformer's destination-side `CLLocation(...)` is not a violation (the rule is about the *user's* position), so a reviewer doesn't fail a correct implementation.
- **Also corrected while in the file, both stale rather than wrong-by-design:** the header's `Status:` line still read "not yet reviewed" — it now names all four AGREE sign-offs with their commits. §12 item 1 (`MKMapItem.forCurrentLocation()` on the iOS 26 SDK) is struck through as **resolved** — `ios-developer` verified it live against current Apple docs, and also confirmed `MKDirections.Request` still carries exactly one source and one destination after WWDC25, so D1/D2's chained legs are the only option, not a workaround. §4.3's fallback is retained as a build-time contingency only.
- **Evidence:** no code written or run — this is a design document amendment. Every claim about the existing app was checked against source this session (`MapScreen.swift`'s `@State` store block and its `.environment(...)` call site, `SavedPlacesStore.swift`, the `scenePhase` `onChange` block that `densityStore`/`eventStore` already use, `PassengerApp.swift` confirming there is no app-level store layer to hang a singleton on). Internal consistency of the two new §9 rows re-read after writing: two self-contradictions found in my own first draft of row 14(b) and row 11(a) and fixed before commit.

### 2026-08-04 — ios-developer — T-057/PAS-46 trd-review of C1–C9 (client-side half): **AGREE**

- **Did:** reviewed `passenger-brain/prds/scenic-walk/TRD.md` (`passenger-brain 987b949`) as the `ios-developer` half of the client review pair (`ios-code-reviewer` covers the other half — already posted its own AGREE with 5 findings before this review started; `data-engineer`+`code-reviewer` cover A1 separately). Read `passenger-code/CLAUDE.md`, this file's Current Snapshot + recent worklog, `prds/scenic-walk/scenic-walk.md`, and the full TRD first. Cross-checked the TRD's claims against the actual source rather than trusting its own description: `Location/LocationStore.swift`, `Support/DirectionsService.swift`, `Hoods/Hood.swift`, `Places/Place.swift`, `Map/HoodLayer.swift`, `Detail/DetailRouter.swift`, `Detail/PlaceDetailModal.swift`, `Hoods/HoodHitTester.swift` all read directly — every one checked out exactly as described (LocationStore's "no coordinates, ever" doc comment is real; `HoodLayer`'s stroke constants match §4.9's table verbatim; `Hood`/`Place` already carry every field §4.5's filter chain reads; `HoodHitTester` is a real working precedent for the pure-`Sendable`-geometry pattern `RouteCorridor`/`ScenicWaypointPlanner` are asked to follow).
- **Verified the two load-bearing MapKit claims against current Apple docs/dev-forum evidence rather than assuming from training data (iOS 26 is fast-moving and the agent contract requires this):** (1) `MKDirections.Request` genuinely still carries exactly one source + one destination as of WWDC25's MapKit additions — no framework-level multi-waypoint support exists even after this cycle's new geocoding/cycling-directions APIs — so D1/D2's chained-2-leg approach is confirmed the only option, not an unnecessary workaround. (2) `MKMapItem.forCurrentLocation()` is confirmed live and still the recommended MapKit pattern for handing `MKDirections` an origin without the app ever touching a `CLLocation` — no fallback needed, §4.3's contingency path is moot.
- **Two concrete pre-C3 findings posted to `PAS-46`, neither blocking `build` but both needing a fix before C3 is coded:** (1) **§4.1's `WalkingRouteProvider` protocol signature is a real Swift 6 strict-concurrency risk.** `MKMapItem` is widely reported as **not `Sendable`**, and the protocol as written (`protocol WalkingRouteProvider: Sendable { func leg(from: MKMapItem, to: MKMapItem) async throws -> RouteLeg }`) passes it across two boundaries this feature needs — the `@MainActor` model calling the provider, and §4.6's two-legs-run-concurrently `async let`/`TaskGroup`, which is the harder crossing of the two. Under `SWIFT_STRICT_CONCURRENCY = complete` (confirmed the project's setting) this either fails to compile or pressures the implementer toward `@unchecked Sendable`, which `passenger-code/CLAUDE.md`'s engineering pillars ban outright. Fix is narrow: have the protocol take `CLLocationCoordinate2D` (+ optional name `String`, both already proven `Sendable` in this codebase per `Hood.swift`'s own conformance note) and build the `MKMapItem` locally inside the conformer's async body, never receiving one as a cross-boundary parameter — preserves D3's privacy contract unchanged. (2) **§4.4 and §4.7 contradict each other on who owns the 5-minute session memo.** §4.4 says `RoutePreviewModel` is "one instance per place-modal presentation... torn down on dismiss" *and* "holds no state across places except the memo" — both can't be true, since a torn-down instance can't carry the memo forward to the next presentation, and the memo (keyed on `Place.id`, TTL 5 min) is exactly what §9 row 10's "+0 requests on re-open within TTL" pass condition depends on. Needs a named, longer-lived owner before C3 — e.g. a small `RouteMemoStore`, environment-injected, same shape as `SavedPlacesStore`.
- **One minor risk named, not a new objection — already partly acknowledged by the TRD itself:** §4.6's 2.5s `resolveBudget` is enforced *between* candidate attempts, not via mid-flight cancellation of an in-flight `MKDirections` call, so a single slow (not throttled) leg could still push wall-clock past 3s; §9 row 7(c)'s BLOCKED-if-throttled condition already covers this honestly. This overlaps with `ios-code-reviewer`'s finding 1 on the same gap — independently arrived at, not copied.
- **Concurred with the TRD's own two flagged trd-review questions (§12 items 1–2):** #1 (`forCurrentLocation` availability) resolved, no fallback needed. #2 (main map vs. mini-map rendering, D9) — agree with the TRD's reading; Hood outlines exist only on `HoodLayer`/the main map, so a second `Map` instance inside the sheet couldn't satisfy req 1's own distinguishability bullet. Still flagged for Aviran/`product`'s explicit confirmation since it's a UX reading, not because it's technically ambiguous.
- **Verdict: AGREE, C1–C9 ready for `build`**, contingent on Findings 1–2 landing as documentation-only TRD amendments before C3 (same treatment T-036/T-034 used, no full re-review owed) — recommended, not required to reopen `trd-review`. Did not move board/Linear state — `code-reviewer`'s A1 (fixture-side backend) verdict is still outstanding; `data-engineer`'s A1 half already landed (AGREE, `passenger-brain 5bfee4d`).
- **Evidence:** Linear comment posted to `PAS-46` (full text there). No `passenger-code` changes made — review only, no build work in this session.
- **Left behind:** two contract-level fixes for whoever codes C3 (`WalkingRouteProvider`'s Sendable-safe signature, the session memo's owner) — either patched into the TRD first or carried as build-time notes; either is fine per the T-036/T-034 precedent. `code-reviewer`'s A1 verdict is the last piece needed before `trd-review` as a whole can clear and the task can move to `build`.
- **Git:** `passenger-brain/agent-os/PROGRESS.md` (this entry). Explicit path, `git diff --staged` re-read before commit per rule 2 — no other session's in-flight hunk on this file at commit time. Not pushed — Aviran-gated, `CLAUDE.md` rule 9. Hash in the final response.

### 2026-08-04 — ios-code-reviewer — T-057/PAS-46 trd-review of C1–C9 (client-side half): **AGREE**

- **Did:** reviewed `passenger-brain/prds/scenic-walk/TRD.md` (`passenger-brain 987b949`) as the `ios-code-reviewer` half of the trd-review pair (`ios-developer` covers the other half; `data-engineer`+`code-reviewer` review A1 separately, out of this agent's scope). Read `passenger-code/CLAUDE.md`, this file's Current Snapshot + recent worklog, `prds/scenic-walk/scenic-walk.md`, and the full TRD before forming a verdict. Cross-checked the TRD's claims about existing code against the actual files rather than trusting its own description — `Passenger/Location/LocationStore.swift`, `Passenger/Support/DirectionsService.swift`, `Passenger/Map/HoodLayer.swift`, all read directly. All three checked out exactly as the TRD describes them: `LocationStore` genuinely holds no coordinate (doc comment confirms it, and there's no `CLLocation` anywhere in the file); `DirectionsService` already carries the iOS 26 `MKPlacemark`→`MKMapItem(location:address:)` migration in its own comment, which independently corroborates the TRD's D3 concern (whether `MKMapItem.forCurrentLocation()` survives the same SDK cycle) is a real, not hypothetical, build risk; `HoodLayer`'s stroke constants (0.5/2.5/3pt, dash `[6,4]`, `Color("Flag")`) match §4.9's line-channel table verbatim.
- **Assessed for App Store/HIG compliance risk (the extra gate this agent carries over `code-reviewer`):** MapKit `MKDirections` chosen over Google Directions (D1) is correct and lowest-risk — free, no account, no API key in the client, MapKit attribution is automatic. No in-app turn-by-turn/voice/rerouting (unchanged decision #19). No waypoint carried into the Apple Maps hand-off, disclosed unconditionally pre-tap (D12) — no dark-pattern risk. No background location, no new location purpose beyond what's already declared for the existing permission flow, no ATT surface (no IDFA/tracking of any kind). §3.3's privacy contract (never receives a `CLLocation`, no persistence, no logging, memo keyed on `Place.id` alone, enforced by §9 row 11's greps with a positive control) is the strongest part of the document.
- **Five findings posted to `PAS-46`, none blocking `build`:** (1) §4.6's 2.5s `resolveBudget` is checked *between* candidate attempts in the flow (§5), not enforced *within* one — no per-leg timeout/race wraps an individual `MKDirections` call, so a single slow (not throttled) response has no forced cutoff and PRD req 7's 3s is a soft target rather than the "structural" guarantee §4.6 claims; recommend a timeout race in `WalkingRouteProvider`/`RoutePreviewModel.resolve`. (2) §4.9's camera bottom-inset is justified by a single hardcoded iPhone 17 QA measurement (y 415–866) — TRD should make explicit that C6 reads the actual rendered sheet frame at runtime, not a device-specific constant, since Dynamic Type and iPad size classes both change sheet height. (3) §9 has no row for RouteControls' 44×44 touch targets, VoiceOver labels, or Dynamic Type, unlike every other interactive control in this codebase (`ProfileButton`, `SearchButton`) — worth an explicit accessibility-label check given the controls carry duration/distance/`via <Hood>` text. (4) The one-time camera fit animation (§4.9) doesn't address Reduce Motion. (5) D3 (`MKMapItem.forCurrentLocation()` on iOS 26) is correctly flagged as trd-review's #1 item by the TRD itself — agreed it should be confirmed at build (C3) rather than block review now, since the named fallback preserves the privacy contract either way.
- **Verdict: AGREE.** C1–C9 is architecturally sound, privacy-correct, and low App Store risk; the five findings are additions/clarifications for `ios-developer` to carry into `build` (1/2/4 into C3/C6, 3 into C5/C8's test pass, 5 already TRD's own open question), not grounds to send the TRD back.
- **Left behind:** `chief-of-staff` should confirm the `ios-developer` half of this review pair has also landed before moving T-057's client surface to `build`. The five findings are non-blocking notes, not tracked as separate follow-up tickets — they belong in the `ios-developer`'s build-time judgment calls per the TRD's own §12 pattern.
- **Git:** `passenger-brain/agent-os/PROGRESS.md` (this entry). Explicit path, re-read `git diff --staged` before committing per rule 2 (shared file). Not pushed — Aviran-gated, `CLAUDE.md` rule 9. Hash in the final chat response. Linear: comment posted to `PAS-46` with the full verdict and findings (no label change — this agent isn't the one that swaps `owner:*` labels per the established Linear-write convention; `chief-of-staff` reconciles once both review-pair halves report).

### 2026-08-04 — code-reviewer — T-057/PAS-46 trd-review, code-reviewer half (A1 fixture-authoring step): **AGREE**

- **Did:** reviewed `prds/scenic-walk/TRD.md` (`passenger-brain 987b949`) §3.4/§10 A1/§12 — the data-model/migration half of `trd-review`, paired with `data-engineer` per the TRD's own §12 review-pair split. Read the PRD, the TRD in full, `database/README.md`'s Status table, and both `006_`/`007_hoods_tel_aviv_data.sql` before forming a view. This is a design-time review — no A1 build has happened yet; `data-engineer` hasn't authored the fixture or regenerated `007` for this task.
- **Question 1 — is authoring through `database/data/hoods-tel-aviv.source.json` + regenerating via `build_hoods.py` safe/correct? AGREE.** This is the exact, already-proven path: `006`→`007`'s prior regeneration (T-035's `is_tourist_trap` seed, T-037's `designated_for_progression` seed) used this identical mechanism and both passed code-review clean. `database/README.md` states plainly "Generated — do not hand-edit; re-run `build_hoods.py` instead," and `hoods-tel-aviv.json` in `passenger-code/Passenger/Resources/` carries the same warning. The generator's own round-trip check (re-run to a scratch path, diff against committed artifacts) is the correct verification and is already this project's standing convention (used by every prior Hood-data change, including T-047 today). No objection.
- **Question 2 — regenerate `007` in place vs. add `008`? AGREE with the TRD's recommendation: regenerate `007` in place.** Reasoning:
  - `database/README.md`'s own rule is "never renumbered and **never edited once applied**." Both `006` and `007` are confirmed **not applied** (`database/README.md`'s Status table, re-checked this session) — the prohibition doesn't bind either file today.
  - There is direct precedent in this exact codebase for editing an unapplied migration in place rather than stacking a new one: `002_synthetic_density_generator.sql`'s 2026-07-30 security fix was "file not applied yet, edited directly rather than a new migration" (`database/README.md`, `002`'s row). Regenerating `007` for A1's fixture flags is the same move.
  - `006` was itself superseded by `007` through exactly this workflow (new migration number, same table, old file kept and marked superseded) — so this isn't a new precedent, it's the second use of an established one.
  - An `008` re-seeding the same table a third time, when neither prior seed has ever been live, adds a file with no historical value to preserve — sequential numbering matters for tracking what a production database has already run, and nothing has run yet. Aviran will apply whichever seed is current at apply-time regardless of how many were written in between.
  - One condition, not a new one: the regenerated `007` must keep `006` intact and marked superseded (already the pattern) and `database/README.md`'s Status table must be updated in the same commit — mechanical, and `data-engineer` has done this correctly on every prior Hood-data change including today's T-047.
- **No RLS or schema concern:** A1 only changes `is_tourist_trap` attribute values on existing rows via the same upsert-based, city-scoped, idempotent migration shape already reviewed and approved at `007`'s original landing (2026-08-03). No new table, column, or policy is in scope for A1.
- **Not this review's to resolve, restated from the TRD:** whether the three specific Hoods A1 proposes (`old-north`/`montefiore`/`shapira`, per TRD §3.4) genuinely read as "not tourist-heavy" is a data judgment for `data-engineer`/Aviran, not a migration-safety question — TRD §3.4 and §12 item 4 already say this themselves.
- **Verdict: AGREE**, no objection to either the authoring path or the regenerate-in-place call. This is the `code-reviewer` half of the trd-review sign-off only — the `ios-developer`/`ios-code-reviewer` pair (C1–C9) and `data-engineer`'s own half of this A1 pair have not both reported back yet, so the task stays at `trd-review` pending those.
- **Left behind:** when `data-engineer` actually authors the A1 fixture and regenerates `007`, the resulting diff (new `is_tourist_trap` flags, new source digest, updated `database/README.md` row) is a fresh, small, mechanical follow-up review — not blocked by anything raised here.
- **Git:** `passenger-brain/agent-os/BOARD.md` (T-057 row, code-reviewer's A1 verdict noted), `agent-os/PROGRESS.md` (this entry). Explicit paths, re-read `git diff --staged` on both shared files before committing per rule 2. Committed, not pushed — Aviran-gated, `CLAUDE.md` rule 9. Hash in the final chat response. **Linear: not posted by this agent** — `chief-of-staff` is the sole Linear writer per `CLAUDE.md`'s "The company" section.
