### 2026-08-04 — architect — T-057/`PAS-46` Scenic Walk (lighter version): TRD written, `trd` → `trd-review`

- **Did:** wrote `prds/scenic-walk/TRD.md` from the approved PRD (Draft v1, `product`, 2026-08-04) against decision #44. Updated T-057's `BOARD.md` row to `trd-review`. Grilling gate **skipped deliberately** — dispatched autonomously with no interactive founder in the loop (`architect.md`: never answer your own questions); every call that would have gone to Aviran is carried as **[ASSUMPTION]** and listed in TRD §12.
- **Surface split (what chief-of-staff needs to dispatch on):** **[iOS] for steps C1–C9, [Algo/Data] for step A1, and no [Backend] step at all** — no schema, no column, no RLS, no endpoint, no Passenger backend call (§3.1). Confirmed against the code, not inherited from the dispatch brief. `code-reviewer` is still on the review list because A1 re-runs `build_hoods.py`, which emits a SQL seed migration alongside the iOS bundle. **Two review pairs: `ios-developer` + `ios-code-reviewer`; `data-engineer` + `code-reviewer`.**
- **The PRD's four open technical questions, all resolved (§1.1):** (1) **MapKit `MKDirections`, chained — Google Directions rejected** (§6, D1/D2). Google costs money and needs an account, both Aviran-gated; it puts an API key in the client; and its platform terms restrict displaying Directions content on a non-Google map — that last point tagged **[ASSUMPTION]**, recalled rather than re-read this session, and deliberately not load-bearing since the first two decide it alone. One waypoint in V1, not two = 2 chained legs concatenated with a 5 m seam dedup. (2) **Request volume: ≤3 `MKDirections` calls per modal open, 5 worst case, 0 per route-control tap, 0 on re-open within a 5-minute session memo** keyed on `Place.id` alone (§4.7). (3) **The hand-off cannot carry waypoints** — no public API puts an intermediate stop into Apple Maps — so req 5's disclosure is unconditional and pre-tap, never an alert after (§4.8). (4) **Req 8's fixture is Hoods-only** (§3.4, D13).
- **Three things the PRD did not know, each found by reading `passenger-code/` rather than assuming:**
  - **`LocationStore` holds no coordinates by design** — its own doc comment reads "Authorization status only — no coordinates, ever." A route needs an origin. Resolved by using `MKMapItem.forCurrentLocation()` as the `MKDirections` source, so the app never receives or stores a `CLLocation`; route geometry is in-memory for one modal presentation only, no cache key contains a coordinate, and `Routing/` carries no logging (§3.3, §4.3, D3). Whether that API survived the iOS 26 SDK's `MKMapItem` deprecations is `trd-review` question 1, with a fallback path named so the build isn't blocked either way.
  - **The PRD's ranking key is degenerate on today's data.** It proposes ranking candidate Hoods by curated-place count. Counted directly: `places-tel-aviv.json` holds **9 places in exactly 3 Hoods** (`florentin`, `kerem-hateimanim`, `neve-tzedek`, 3 each) and **not one of them is `false`-flagged** — the key is 0 for every eligible candidate. Kept as the primary sort for real data, plus a second non-degenerate key (waypoint distance off-route) and a `Hood.centroid` waypoint fallback for a Hood with no curated place (§4.5, D6).
  - **"Draws its polyline inside the modal's own space" resolves to the main map behind the sheet, not a mini-map** — argued from the PRD's own next bullet, which asks that a route line be distinguishable from a Hood's tourist-trap outline at neighborhood zoom, and Hood outlines are drawn by `HoodLayer` on the main map and nowhere else (§1.2, §4.9, D9). Still tagged **[ASSUMPTION]** and raised as `trd-review` question 2: if `product` meant a map *inside* the modal, §4.9, two §9 rows and step C6 all change.
- **The four PRD [ASSUMPTION]s, adjudicated rather than passed through:** waypoint heuristic **refined** — the corridor is derived from the **fast route's own polyline**, not the straight A→B line, buffer 800 m. Measured, not guessed: on an Old North → Neve Tzedek corridor a 500 m straight-line buffer catches only Hoods the route already runs through (3 m, 5 m, 25 m), with the first genuinely off-route Hood at 433 m — 500 m would empty the candidate set by construction. Detour bounds **ratified as proposed** (1.5× AND +15 min, which is a `min`, not two independent tests) **plus a new 5-minute floor, mine, [ASSUMPTION]**. The 3 s budget **ratified and made structural** — a 2.5 s wall-clock resolve budget plus a 2-candidate attempt cap, so the bound is enforced by the loop rather than hoped for. Modal-only entry point **ratified**.
- **§9 carries 12 verification rows — the 8 P0s plus 4 normative rules pulled out of §2–§5** (the corridor filters, the request-volume caps, the no-location-persisted contract, the camera fit), per L-032. Every negative-existence check carries a named positive control; every ∀ is preceded by an exact count; every visibility row asserts rendered frames rather than `.exists`. **Req 4's "identical polyline" rule is implemented as a divergence test** (≥100 m contiguous run outside the fast route's 25 m corridor), because MapKit returns different point counts for the same path — a byte-equality check would never fire, and the requirement would have passed by never being exercised (D8).
- **Fixture (req 8), specified as constraints plus a computed proposal:** ≥3 mutually non-adjacent `false`-flagged Hoods, non-adjacency defined falsifiably (no vertex pair within 50 m, centroids ≥800 m apart), on one documented demo corridor, provisional-marked per the `florentin` blurb precedent, asserted against `Bundle.main` in the `PassportBundleInvariantTests` style, and verified by re-running the generator to a scratch path and diffing — the only check that proves no hand-edit survived (L-024). Geometric proposal computed from the committed source polygons: `old-north`, `montefiore`, `shapira`; min vertex separations 1136/2865/428 m, centroid separations 2830/4454/2290 m; demo corridor origin `32.0945, 34.7981` (Bavli) → `florentin-anna-loulou-bar`, with `montefiore` 715 m and `shapira` 1102 m off that line, both inside the 250–1200 m waypoint band. **The substance is `data-engineer`'s and Aviran's, not mine** — whether those three neighbourhoods *are* not tourist-heavy is a data claim this TRD has no standing to make, and straight-line geometry must be re-measured against a real `MKDirections` walking route before the fixture is committed.
- **Deliberately did NOT touch `places-tel-aviv.json`** (D13). Adding a curated place to a `false`-flagged Hood would exercise the primary waypoint path on device, but it would also change the shipped seed that `SearchShippedSeedTests` and `search-quick-filters/TRD.md` §9 row 3 assert **exact arrays** against — real breakage in another feature's passing tests, for coverage a pure unit fixture gives for free. Consequence stated in the TRD rather than hidden: the on-device Phase-1 demo exercises the centroid fallback, and §9 row 8(d) says so.
- **Left behind — one new risk, for Aviran/`product`, that the architecture cannot solve (§8 R1):** "not flagged tourist-heavy" is being used as a proxy for "worth walking through," and it is not one. Decision #37 retired the graduated tag, so `isTouristTrap == false` is the entire basis of the recommendation in V1 — nothing in this design would stop a `false` flag on, say, the old central-bus-station area from becoming a recommended detour. Either accept that, or add a second gate before real Phase-2 data drives it.
- **Also left behind, unchanged from the PRD and still Aviran's:** `strategy/passenger-strategy.md`'s V1-scope navigation bullet still describes decision #32's weighted per-segment routing and now contradicts what this TRD specifies. Aviran-gated file — **reported, not edited** (`CLAUDE.md` rule 8). And req 5's disclosed gap: the scenic detour cannot survive the hand-off, so a user can preview one walk and take another; §4.8 makes the disclosure unconditional and pre-tap, but whether that trade is acceptable is his call. One question for `code-reviewer` rather than Aviran: `006_` and `007_hoods_tel_aviv_data.sql` both already seed the same table and neither is applied — the TRD recommends regenerating `007` in place rather than adding an `008`, as a recommendation, not a ruling.
- **Evidence:** all geometry, flag counts and place counts in the TRD were computed this session from the committed artifacts (`passenger-brain/database/data/hoods-tel-aviv.source.json`, `passenger-code/Passenger/Resources/places-tel-aviv.json`), not carried from the PRD. `HoodLayer`'s stroke constants used in §4.9's line-channel argument (0.5 / 2.5 / 3 pt, dash `[6, 4]`, `Color("Flag")`) were read from `passenger-code/Passenger/Map/HoodLayer.swift` directly. No `passenger-code` changes made by this session.

### 2026-08-04 — product — T-036/PAS-27 Places — Been & Saved, acceptance (round 8): **ACCEPT** → `aviran-review`

- **Did:** acceptance pass against `prds/places-been-saved/places-been-saved.md` + `TRD.md`. Read `passenger-brain/CLAUDE.md`, this file's recent worklog (qa's round-8 entry, chief-of-staff's five-dispatch run, the T-032 founder-override stub) and both `BOARD.md` T-036 rows before touching anything. **Every P0 re-derived from `passenger-code` source directly** — not from qa's PASS WITH MINORS or `ios-code-reviewer`'s round-7 APPROVE — per this workspace's standing acceptance convention.
- **Re-ran the suites instead of inheriting them, and this mattered:** qa tested `passenger-code 92561a7`, but **two commits have landed since and one of them touched this feature** — `c1b8bc3` (PAS-48) changed `PlacesListOverlay`'s `.frame(maxHeight:)` from 420 to 480, and `5e1f72f` fixed a standalone-build break in `c1b8bc3`. Built and tested a detached worktree at **`5e1f72f`** on its own `-derivedDataPath`: **`PassengerTests` 401/401 in 71 suites, TEST SUCCEEDED**; **`PassengerUITests/PlacesListInteractionTests` 6/6, 0 failures, TEST SUCCEEDED**. Worktree and DerivedData removed afterwards; `git status`/`git worktree list` confirm the shared `passenger-code` tree's HEAD and its six other sessions' uncommitted files are exactly as I found them — I staged and wrote nothing there.
- **Scope gate re-run on inherited work (Job 0 item 4), PASSED by exact-string grep rather than memory:** both Motivation quotes are verbatim in `strategy/passenger-strategy.md` line 43 (`grep -c` = 1 each) — the `"Been" and "Saved" places, visually/functionally distinct` line and the decision-#38 permanently-closed line. That same strategy line independently authorises req 3's 20-minute dwell threshold and its already-known-place guard, so the requirement is not an agent's invention. No social, onboarding, or Phase-2/3 surface anywhere in the shipped feature.
- **Verdict per requirement, each traced to a file:**
  - **Req 1 — MET.** `PlacesListOverlay.header` titles it "Places". `PlacesListEntry.provenance` is a non-optional `PlaceProvenance`, so "exactly one word, never two, never none" is a property of the type, not a view rule. Precedence is `saved`-first-then-`visits[id]` in `PlacesListComposition.provenance`, i.e. `max` with the branch that cannot lose taken first. One row per place by `compactMap` over the catalog.
  - **Req 2 — MET.** `SavedPlacesStore.toggle` mutates in memory with no `await` (persistence is fire-and-forget behind a generation guard), so the sub-400ms bar is met by construction. `PlaceDetailModal` renders `savedPlaces.isSaved(place.id)` directly off the store, never a `@State` mirror. Un-save falling to `.been`, or dropping the row when no visit exists, is unit-tested both ways.
  - **Req 3 — MET on bullets 3 and 4; bullets 1 and 2 are NOT satisfiable in Build Phase 1 and the PRD now says so.** No confirmation UI exists anywhere in the feature; a duplicate `place_id` keeps the higher `VisitKind` (`BundledVisitSource.keepHigherKind`), so one place can never produce two rows. The 20-minute threshold and the known-place guard describe a detector that does not exist — Been/Visited is a bundled read-only fixture (TRD D2). See the PRD correction below.
  - **Req 4 — MET.** The save path has no closed-state condition, so saving a closed place succeeds. `PlacesListRow.closedBadge` renders iff `place.permanentlyClosed`, as a `Capsule` with `nosign`, never red. Closed state is never copied into `saved-places.json` (slugs only) or the visit fixture (slug + kind), which is what makes "badges on next render" true **regardless of T-044's ownerless refresh**. `Directions` is `.disabled(availableApps.isEmpty)` and nothing else — confirmed live, not by read, by `testClosedPlaceRowOpensDetailWithDirectionsEnabledNotBlocked` on `neve-nachum-gutman-museum`. The two-surface independence in bullet 3 holds structurally: `PlaceDetailModal.touristTrapSlot` conditions on `isTouristTrap == true`, the badge on `permanentlyClosed` — separate fields, separate `if`s, no shared copy.
  - **Req 5 — MET as scoped.** Re-grepped the feature's files rather than trusting their doc comments: zero `CoreLocation` imports, zero authorization reads, zero permission requests, and `VisitedPlacesStore` has no write path. The list opens and Saved rows render with no location dependency of any kind.
  - **Req 6 — MET.** `PlacesListEmptyState`'s line reads "Save a place from its detail card, or spend enough time at one, and it'll show up here." — it names both ways a place gets into the list, which is what the round-6 REJECT was about. No network path exists in this feature, so the offline bullet is satisfied by absence, consistent with the struck sync clause and D5.
  - **Req 7 — MET.** `PlaceLayer.isListed` is a plain `Bool`, so provenance has no channel onto the map. The whole `ForEach` is gated on the existing `showsNames`, so no second zoom constant exists to drift. The ring is **dashed** (shape, not colour alone) at 56pt with `.allowsHitTesting(false)`, outside an unchanged 44×44 frame. No clustering is built, so the cluster bullet is vacuously satisfied.
  - **Req 8 — MET.** `onSelect` calls `detailRouter.openPlace(place)` only — the Hood sheet is skipped. `MapChromeState.toggle` swaps rather than stacks. Camera and `selectedHour` are neither read nor written anywhere in this feature, and all three dismissal paths return to an unchanged map (UI-tested).
- **Two PRD corrections landed in the same commit as the verdict (L-009), plus the `Status:` flip (L-006):**
  1. **Req 5's correction was already ruled "owed" on 2026-08-03 and never written into the Requirements section** — it lived only in the Decisions log, where no gate reads it. Applied now: in Build Phase 1 "Been and Visited never populate" is verified as the *absence of any sensor path*, not as runtime behaviour, because the fixture populates regardless of permission; re-run against the real detector at T-046/`PAS-33`.
  2. **Req 3 had the same defect and nobody had named it.** Its first two bullets had no honest pass condition at this build phase and the carry-forward lived only in the TRD's §9 row 3. Both now carry explicit build-phase scope naming T-046/`PAS-33`. Stated as scope, not as a softening — the bullets are unchanged.
  3. `Status:` flipped Draft v1 → **Accepted 2026-08-04 (Build Phase 1)**, so the spec stops contradicting reality and the nightly PM audit stops re-flagging it.
- **qa's D8 finding — weighed, and passed through rather than blocking, with reasons.** The TRD's D8 *rationale* text is wrong: it claims `.presentationBackgroundInteraction(.enabled(upThrough: .medium))` makes bucket-2 chrome tappable while a sheet is up, and qa measured that the nav row (y 700–744) sits entirely inside the `.medium` sheet's frame (y 415–866), so a real tap there hits the sheet. But **req 8 bullet 2 asks for mutual exclusivity, not for that tappability**, and exclusivity holds — `handlePresentedSurfaceChange` closes a stacked place modal on leaving `.places`, covered by both `MapScreenTests` and a live UI test that passed at `5e1f72f`. Rejecting the build over prose in a document the build correctly implements would be rejecting work against a stale spec. **Filed as `BOARD.md` T-058, owner `architect`** — a row with an owner, not a worklog line (`CLAUDE.md` rule 5) — bundled with the same file's stale D7, which `ios-code-reviewer` flagged on 2026-08-04 and which never became a row either.
- **Evidence:** `PassengerTests` 401/401 and `PlacesListInteractionTests` 6/6 at `passenger-code 5e1f72f`; logs in this session's scratchpad, not committed (not project artifacts). Scope-gate greps against `strategy/passenger-strategy.md` line 43.
- **Left behind:** (1) **T-058** for `architect` — TRD D7/D8 prose. (2) T-046/`PAS-33` now carries two named re-verification obligations from this PRD (req 3 bullets 1–2, req 5 bullet 1) rather than one implied one. (3) **No shipped seed row is both `permanently_closed` and `is_tourist_trap`**, so req 4 bullet 3's independence is verified structurally rather than by a rendered example — noted in the PRD's risks so a later gate doesn't assume a render happened. An **uncommitted** working-tree edit by another session flips `kerem-carmel-spice-corner`'s `is_tourist_trap` to `true`, which would create exactly that combination; not mine to commit or to judge, flagged for whoever owns it. Without a commit there's no diff for `ios-code-reviewer` to review and no commit hash to cite per the single-writer Linear protocol's mandatory-comment rule — so I did not add an `owner:*` label or move status past `Backlog`, and did not relay-dispatch anyone yet.
- **Left behind:** once the 4 fixes are committed (by the reporting session or whoever picks this up), claim each ticket (`owner:ios-code-reviewer`, status → `In Progress`) and relay-dispatch `ios-code-reviewer` against the actual commit hash(es), per the single-writer claim protocol. No BOARD.md rows added — these are Linear-only, same convention as PAS-42 (bug-ticket-style small fixes aren't tracked as BOARD.md `T-xxx` rows).

### 2026-08-04 — qa — T-036/PAS-27 QA pass (round 8, post round-7 `ios-code-reviewer` APPROVE): **PASS WITH MINORS**

- **Did:** fresh behavioral QA pass against `places-been-saved.md`/`TRD.md`, per round-7 code-review's brief to verify — live, not by code read — the three Places-list dismissal paths, the D8 co-presentation guard, the F1 empty-state copy fix, and the closed badge never disabling Directions. Read `passenger-code/CLAUDE.md`, this file's recent worklog, and `BOARD.md`'s T-036 row before starting. Updated `prds/places-been-saved/TEST-PLAN.md` with a new dated section (existing rows 1–3/4a–c/5/7 unchanged, not re-derived).
- **Environment (not a product finding, disclosed since it shaped the method):** manual `mcp__Claude_Code_iOS_Simulator__control` tap/`touch_path` interaction was unreliable for 30+ minutes — `uptime` load averages of 200–800 on this 10-core shared machine, confirmed via `ps aux` showing multiple other sessions' concurrent `xcodebuild test` processes (one named `T037-review`). One full `xcodebuild test` run's `PassengerUITests-Runner` hit "test runner timed out while preparing to run tests" on first attempt (environment, not code — retry succeeded 19/19 clean). Manual taps stayed unreliable even after load dropped to single digits, while XCUITest's own synthesized touches (in-process via the linked XCTest framework, not external simulator touch injection) worked reliably throughout every attempt.
- **Response to the environment issue:** rather than force a BLOCKED verdict on the four briefed items, wrote a live UI test (`PassengerUITests/PlacesListInteractionTests.swift`, `passenger-code 92561a7`, 6 tests) that exercises exactly what a manual click-through would have — this closes the "live, not code read" gap the reviewer asked for and leaves permanent regression coverage behind instead of a one-off. All 6 pass against current `main` (`0e3b3dc`+this commit): closed-badge row (`neve-nachum-gutman-museum`) opens the detail modal with `Directions` enabled (never blocked); all three dismissal paths (✕, scrim tap, drag past the 80pt threshold) each return to an unchanged map; D8 direction 1 (opening Places over an open Heat modal replaces it) confirmed live; D8 direction 2 (dismissing a stacked place modal reveals the list unchanged, then leaving `.places` for Heat closes it) confirmed live, after adjusting the test to a physically reachable path — see finding below.
- **Finding (Minor, non-blocking, pre-existing — not this task's to fix):** the first version of the D8-direction-2 test tried a raw tap on `Heat` while a place-detail modal was stacked over the list, and it failed. The xcresult accessibility snapshot showed why: the depth-1 `.sheet` at `.medium` occupies `{{8, 415}, {386, 451}}` (y 415–866); `MapNavRow`'s buttons (all 5, post-PAS-42) sit at y 700–744, entirely inside that span — a real tap there hits the sheet, not what's structurally underneath, regardless of `.presentationBackgroundInteraction(.enabled(upThrough: .medium))`, which keeps the *state* reachable but doesn't make a visually-covered button tappable. This contradicts the TRD's own D8 rationale text ("makes bucket-2 chrome tappable while a sheet is up... both orderings are reachable states, not hypotheticals"). **Pre-existing** — the same geometry held for the original bucket-2 `padding(.bottom, 32)` position before PAS-42 moved Places into the always-visible row, so this is not a regression T-036 or PAS-42 introduced, and D8's actual contract (mutual exclusivity, nothing ever stranded) holds via the reachable path (dismiss the stacked modal first, then switch). Flagged for `architect`/`product` to correct the D8 rationale text or reconsider chrome placement relative to sheet coverage.
- **Row 6 (empty state) — not re-confirmed by on-device render this pass.** `PlacesListEmptyState.swift`'s line (`"Save a place from its detail card, or spend enough time at one, and it'll show up here."`) is confirmed by direct source read, matching PRD req 6 bullet 1 and the F1 fix — same standard round-7's own review applied ("by direct read, not by trusting the commit message"). Could not render it live: Build Phase 1's bundled visit fixture always ships 4 entries (D2 — populates unconditionally), so the app's default state is never actually empty, and there's no launch-argument hook (unlike `-uiTestZoomedIn`) to force it. Adding one was judged a production-code change outside this pass's remit, not something to improvise under time pressure. This is a structural Build-Phase-1 fact, not a new gap.
- **Full suite, confirmed twice:** first full run hit the UI-test-runner timeout above (environment); full suite re-run clean on `passenger-code 92561a7` (dedicated simulator, own `-derivedDataPath`, per L-029): **PassengerTests 401/401, PassengerUITests 25/25 (19 pre-existing + 6 new), 0 failures, 0 skipped.** `BUILD SUCCEEDED` both times.
- **Verdict: PASS WITH MINORS.** All P0 cases this pass targeted were run (none blocked/unrun — the empty-state row's source-confirmation is consistent with round-7's own standard for the same string, not a case left unexecuted). One Minor, non-blocking, pre-existing finding filed above. Moving T-036/PAS-27 to `acceptance` per the QA lifecycle rule.
- **Evidence:** `passenger-brain/prds/places-been-saved/TEST-PLAN.md` (new dated section, this pass). `passenger-code 92561a7` (`PassengerUITests/PlacesListInteractionTests.swift`, 6 tests). Full build+test logs and the xcresult accessibility snapshot backing the D8 finding kept in this session's scratchpad, not committed (not project artifacts).
- **Left behind:** the D8-rationale-vs-reachability finding, for `architect`/`product`. Row 6's genuine on-device empty-state render, for whoever next has reason to add a `-uiTestEmptyPlaces`-style hook (or accept the source-read standard as sufficient, consistent with round-7's own precedent).
- **Git:** `passenger-brain` — `prds/places-been-saved/TEST-PLAN.md`, `agent-os/PROGRESS.md` (this entry). `passenger-code` — `92561a7` (new test file only, explicit path staged, verified `git diff --staged --stat` before committing; did not touch the shared tree's other uncommitted files — `chief-of-staff.md`, `EventDetailModal.swift`, `EventDetailRows.swift`, `HeatModalCard.swift`, `HourReadout.swift`, `MapScreen.swift`, two seed JSONs, `EventDetailRowsTests.swift`, and the untracked `HeatModalCardLayoutTests.swift` — all confirmed still present and unstaged after my commit). Not pushed — Aviran-gated, `CLAUDE.md` rule 9. Hashes in the final chat response.

### 2026-08-04 — product — T-057/`PAS-46` Scenic Walk lighter version: PRD written, `spec` → `trd`

- **Did:** wrote `prds/scenic-walk/scenic-walk.md` (Draft v1, 8 P0s + 2 P1s) against decision #44. Created Linear **`PAS-46`** — "Scenic Walk — lighter version (route-preview comparison)", project Passenger V1, team PAS, `Todo`, labels `Feature` + `owner:architect`, standard template (Description / Motivation / Requirements / DoD / Links), PRD linked. Added the `prds/INDEX.md` row and updated T-057's `BOARD.md` row to `trd`/`architect`. `prds/scenic-walk/feasibility.md` left in place untouched, as instructed.
- **Scope gate — PASSED, verified by exact-string grep not by memory:** two verbatim lines from `strategy/passenger-strategy.md` sit in Motivation — the Phase-2 section's "V1 now ships a route preview only — polyline comparison of scenic vs. fast, then hand-off to native Maps/Waze for the actual walk", and the V1-scope line "**Navigation — \"Scenic Walk\"** (walking only) and a **fastest-route mode**, user's choice." Both `grep -c` = 1. No social, onboarding, or Phase-2/3 surface; nothing hooks toward the parked weighted-routing version.
- **Build-phase call, made explicitly rather than assumed either way (the brief asked for this):** Build Phase 1, client-side only — no Passenger backend call, no new table, column, endpoint or RLS surface. **One condition, filed as req 8, not left implicit:** the scenic bias reads the tourist-trap flag, and I read the shipped bundle directly (`passenger-code/Passenger/Resources/hoods-tel-aviv.json`) rather than trusting any doc — **21 of 24 Hoods ship `isTouristTrap: null`, 2 `true`, 1 `false`.** As bundled today the scenic path is undemonstrable and would degrade to "no scenic route" everywhere, silently. Req 8 requires ≥3 non-adjacent `false` Hoods on a documented demo corridor plus a bundle-asserting test, authored through `database/data/hoods-tel-aviv.source.json` and regenerated per L-024. **That is authoring, not a Build-Phase-2 dependency** — real-data flag quality improves in Phase 2; the feature does not wait on it.
- **Inherited-work gate (Job 0 item 4) re-run on `feasibility.md`'s data addendum:** confirmed the lighter version needs no new data and no supporting data PRD — `hood-dataset` req 5 (`polygon`, tourist-trap flag) and `places-dataset` req 1 (coordinates) already deliver every field. Its named trap — a heuristic treating `null` as `false` routes through unrated Hoods and calls them local — is now req 2's own pass/fail bullet, not a note someone has to remember.
- **Four `[ASSUMPTION]`s carried, deliberately unresolved:** the waypoint-selection heuristic (corridor → `false`-flagged Hoods → rank by curated-place count → nearest curated place as the waypoint, since a centroid can land somewhere unwalkable); the 1.5×/+15-minute detour bounds; the 3s resolve budget; and modal-only entry. Aviran specified the shape, not the algorithm (L-013 — I did not read specificity into his quote that isn't in it; the relayed text says only "biased via 1-2 waypoints toward good Hoods").
- **Evidence:** 1,519 words — over the skill's ~800/~1,200 budget, disclosed rather than hidden. It is the second-shortest PRD in `prds/` (the other ten run 1,510–5,785) and every P0 bullet is a falsifiable pass condition, which the budget rule forbids cutting; I compressed prose twice instead of dropping facts. **HTML twin NOT rendered** — the `/feature-prd` skill's `scripts/md-to-html.py` does not exist in this repo (`find` across both repos returns nothing), so the step could not be performed. Reporting it as not-done rather than hand-rolling a substitute (`CLAUDE.md` rule 9). The `.html` is gitignored/local-only, so nothing is missing from `main`; other PRDs' committed-looking `.html` twins predate the script's disappearance.
- **Left behind — two items for Aviran, neither of which an agent may act on:**
  1. **`strategy/passenger-strategy.md` still describes the heavier version.** Its V1-scope navigation bullet reads as decision #32's weighted per-segment Attractiveness routing and still carries its own `[FLAGGED — scope increase]` note. Decision #44 supersedes it in `decisions.md`, but the **source of truth now contradicts what we are building.** The file is Aviran-gated; reporting, not editing (`CLAUDE.md` rule 8). Suggested replacement text, for Aviran to apply or reject: *"Navigation — **Scenic Walk** (walking only) and a **fastest-route mode**, user's choice. V1 ships the route-preview comparison locked 2026-07-27 (decision #19) and confirmed 2026-08-04 (decision #44): two walking routes from the platform's own directions API, the scenic one biased through 1–2 waypoints toward Hoods not flagged tourist-heavy. Per-street Attractiveness weighting is a fast-follow, not V1. Hands off to native Maps/Waze either way; no in-app turn-by-turn, voice, or rerouting in V1."*
  2. **Req 5's disclosed gap:** the scenic waypoints may not survive the hand-off (Waze in particular). The PRD requires the app to say so before exiting; whether previewing one walk and taking another is acceptable at all is Aviran's call, not `product`'s.
- **Also left behind, for `architect` at `trd`:** `MKDirections.Request` carries one source and one destination, so a 1–2 waypoint route is likely 2–3 chained requests concatenated — confirm, then choose chaining vs. Google Directions (native waypoint support). Also unresolved: request volume/caching per modal open, and `PAS-7`'s still-untested premise that a waypointed route actually *feels* scenic on real Tel Aviv streets (field test before launch, not a code question).
- **Git:** `passenger-brain` — `prds/scenic-walk/scenic-walk.md`, `prds/INDEX.md`, `agent-os/BOARD.md`, `agent-os/PROGRESS.md`, explicit paths. Not pushed (Aviran-gated, `CLAUDE.md` rule 9). `feasibility.md` untouched.

- **Did:** fresh behavioral pass against PAS-42's Definition of Done, live on-device (not a re-read of ios-developer's/ios-code-reviewer's evidence).   - **DoD 1 (single row, default + AX5):** default text size — screenshot confirms all 5 icons (Heat/Search/Profile/NearMe/Places) in one row, centered, no clipping. AX5 (`simctl ui <udid> content_size accessibility-extra-extra-extra-large` set **before first launch**, on a freshly `simctl erase`d device, matching ios-developer's documented working recipe) — confirmed the row itself stays unclipped and correctly positioned; only the SF Symbol glyphs overflow their 44×44 circles, exactly as ios-developer disclosed. Repeated for Search, Heat, and Places: all 5 icons visible in every case, none fade or clip regardless of which surface is open. Screenshots taken for each.

NearMe recenter tested **while Search was open** (the newly-allowed case): set a custom simulator location (`simctl location set`), tapped NearMe with Search still presented — map recentered to the new location correctly, Search sheet stayed open throughout, no crash, no visual glitch.

  - **Spot-check test run (not a full-suite re-run — dev/reviewer already ran 401+404 tests twice; this corroborates the specific PAS-42 surfaces):** `MapScreenD8WiringTests` (contains `reopeningPlacesWhileAlreadyOpenIsANoOp`, the guard's only direct unit test) — 5/5 passed. `HeatButtonInteractionTests` 3/3, `ProfileButtonInteractionTests` 2/2, `SearchAccessibilityTests` 4/4 — all passed at default content size. **Self-inflicted false failure, caught and resolved:** the first `SearchAccessibilityTests` run showed 3 failures (`testHoodResultVoiceOverLabelIsPinnedExactly`, `testPlaceResultVoiceOverLabelIsPinnedExactly`, `testResultRowMeetsMinimumTouchTarget`) — traced to my own leftover AX5 `content_size` setting still active on the simulator from the manual AX5 pass above (`simctl ui <udid> content_size` confirmed it was still `accessibility-extra-extra-extra-large`). Read `MapNavRow.swift`, `MapScreen.swift`, `MapChromeState.swift`, all five icon-button views, `HoodButton.swift`/`SettingsHint.swift`, and all four z5 surface views (`HeatModalCard`, `PlacesListOverlay`, `PassportSurface`, `SearchOverlay`) end to end before touching anything, to understand exactly which z-order/fade mechanics were in play, not just the dispatch brief's line numbers (which had drifted slightly from a same-day, unrelated concurrent edit — read live source, not cited lines).
  - **`MapNavRow.swift`**: now takes `nearMeAuthorizationStatus`/`onNearMeTap`/`onPlacesTap` alongside the existing three, and its `HStack` composes all 5 buttons (Heat, Search, Profile, NearMe, Places) in that order. Header doc comment documents the requirement-#3 decision inline (below).
  - **`MapScreen.swift`**: the z7 overlay now passes NearMe/Places through to `MapNavRow`; the z-bottom overlay that used to hold `SettingsHint`/`HoodButton`/`NearMeButton`/`PlacesButton` together now holds only the first two (unchanged fade behavior, per the ticket's "leave HoodButton/SettingsHint's own stacking alone"). `openPlacesList` gained a guard (below).
  - **`PlacesButton.swift`**: dropped `isFaded`/`.opacity`/`.allowsHitTesting` — the button never fades now.
  - **`PassengerTests/MapScreenTests.swift`**: one new test, `reopeningPlacesWhileAlreadyOpenIsANoOp`, covering the new guard.
- **Requirement #3 (fade/hide rule) — D1 wins for the whole merged row, not D7:** the row stays always-visible/always-hit-testable regardless of which `NavSurface` is open, exactly like the pre-existing Heat/Search/Profile behavior. D7 (fade on any open surface) could not have won row-wide without breaking two already-shipped, already-tested behaviors: `HeatButtonInteractionTests.testTappingHeatButtonAgainDismissesTheModal` and `ProfileButtonInteractionTests.testTappingProfileButtonAgainDismissesPassport` both re-tap their own button *while its own modal is open* and require the tap to land — D7 applied to the whole row would fade those buttons to non-hit-testable the moment their own surface opened, which is a straight regression on shipped, tested behavior. That left one gap: D7's actual job for `PlacesButton` was never "fade" for its own sake, it was "stop Places being re-tapped to dismiss its own list." Preserved that specific protection without the fade, as a guard in `MapScreen.openPlacesList` (`guard chrome.presented != .places else { return }`) — a true no-op on re-tap while already open, not a `chrome.toggle` that would close it. The three other dismissal paths D7 named (✕, drag-past-threshold, tap-outside-scrim) are untouched. Documented inline in both `MapNavRow.swift`'s header comment and `PlacesButton.swift`'s header comment, per the ticket's ask to document it the same way D1/D7 originally were.
- **Evidence:**
  - `xcodebuild build` (own scratch `-derivedDataPath`, dedicated never-booted simulator UDID per L-029) → **BUILD SUCCEEDED**.
  - `xcodebuild test`, same project/scheme/simulator → **TEST SUCCEEDED**: `Test run with 404 tests in 71 suites passed` (Swift Testing, `PassengerTests` — includes the new `MapScreen D8 presentation wiring` suite test), plus `PassengerUITests.xctest`: `Executed 20 tests, with 0 failures` across all 8 UI suites, explicitly including the three the ticket named by name (`HeatButtonInteractionTests` 3/3, `ProfileButtonInteractionTests` 2/2, `SearchAccessibilityTests` 4/4) — none needed a code change, since they look up buttons by accessibility label (`app.buttons["Heat"]` etc.), not by the `MapNavRow` type name.
  - **Single-row layout, rendered, not just unit-tested:** built and installed on a fresh `iPhone SE (3rd generation)` simulator (iOS 26.5) — the narrowest iOS-26-capable iPhone width (375pt logical) available in this environment, chosen because 5×44pt buttons + 4×16pt spacing = 284pt is the widest the row gets and needed the tightest screen to prove it doesn't clip. Screenshot at default text size: all 5 buttons in one row, centered, comfortable margin both sides. Screenshot at AX5 (`xcrun simctl ui <udid> content_size accessibility-extra-extra-extra-large`, set **before** app launch — setting it after first launch or via `-UIPreferredContentSizeCategoryName` launch args didn't reliably take, matching `SearchAccessibilityTests`' own documented note that this mechanism is flaky on this Xcode/iOS build; the `simctl ui` route worked once applied pre-launch and confirmed via `simctl ui <udid> content_size` echoing it back): the row is still one line, still unclipped — expected by construction, since every icon button's outer `.frame(width: 44, height: 44)` is a hardcoded literal, not content-driven, so Dynamic Type cannot change the row's layout width. **Disclosed, not fixed (pre-existing, out of scope):** at AX5 the SF Symbol glyphs inside each button visibly overflow their own 44×44 `.thinMaterial` circle (the `Image`'s `.font(.title3)` scales with Dynamic Type; the frame around it doesn't clip). This is identical, pre-existing per-button styling shared by every icon button before this task (Heat/Search/Profile already had it) — newly visible here only because NearMe/Places now sit in the same dense row, not a regression this task introduced. Not in PAS-42's scope ("no new icon", nothing about individual button styling) — leaving it named here rather than silently noticed and dropped.
  - **Corrects a standing assumption for future sessions:** `SearchAccessibilityTests.swift`'s header comment states neither AX5 mechanism "took visible effect" on this Xcode 17F113/iOS 26.5 build. Same binary versions here, but `simctl ui content_size` **did** take effect when set before the app's first launch after a simulator erase — the difference from that earlier attempt was ordering/erase state, not the tooling itself. Didn't edit that test file's comment (out of scope for this task, and it correctly describes what that session tried), but flagging it here since it's a live contradiction of a documented "simulator/tooling limitation" — worth whoever next hits an AX5 wall trying erase-then-set-before-launch before concluding it's broken again.
  - Full log: `xcodebuild test` output archived nowhere durable (scratch, deleted with the derived data below) — summary lines quoted above are the durable record.
- **Left behind:** nothing blocking. **Cleanup done, not left for someone else:** the scratch simulator (`PAS42-Verify-*`) was shut down and `simctl delete`d, and its `-derivedDataPath` (`.../scratchpad/dd-pas42`) was `rm -rf`'d after the run — `df -h /` confirmed 34Gi free afterward, no leak. One other session's in-flight, unrelated, uncommitted work was live in the same shared working tree during this task (T-052/PAS-40's `EventDetailModal`/`EventDetailRows`/`HeatModalCard`/`HourReadout` changes, plus T-051/PAS-39's fixture files and two new UI/unit test files) — confirmed via `git diff` that I never wrote to any of those files, and used `git add -p` on `MapScreen.swift` specifically to stage only my 4 hunks and leave that session's 1 hunk (the `EventDetailModal(event:hoodName:)` change inside the same file) unstaged, rather than sweeping it in under an explicit-path commit (`passenger-brain/CLAUDE.md` rule 2's exact warning). Nothing else that session touched was ever staged.

### 2026-08-03 — retrospective — nightly run covering 2026-08-03 itself: 4 new lessons (L-030 to L-033), all applied; L-009 marked failed and re-fixed at the authoring stage; 6 past fixes verified

- **Did:** the nightly retro. **Window:** `LESSONS.md`'s three prior `2026-08-03` headers all covered other windows (the nightly one covered 08-02's 19:20 tail; L-028 and L-029 were single-investigation targeted runs), so **no run had covered 2026-08-03's own work** — 73 `passenger-brain` commits, 22 `passenger-code` commits, `chief-of-staff` rounds 3–9, four features through review/QA/acceptance, the day's first two acceptance REJECTs. Read `LESSONS.md` in full, `BOARD.md`, this file's 2026-08-03 worklog, both repos' `git log --stat` since midnight, all 25 Linear issues with activity today, and tonight's PM audit digest on `PAS-32` (posted 20:20, available).
- **Four lessons, all applied.** Not four separate problems — three are one structural fact seen from three angles: the fleet now runs enough concurrent sessions that shared resources, gate tokens and claim locks all fail at scale in ways they did not at three sessions a day.
  - **L-030** (qa-testing, general) — a gate verdict is a claim about coverage as much as about defects, and only the token travels downstream. T-032/PAS-15 cleared `qa` as PASS WITH MINORS with **11 of 27 cases never executed**, and `product` then REJECTed it on two blocking findings, one of them exactly the blocked AX5 row. Same night, `ios-code-reviewer` returned APPROVE WITH MINORS on T-038/PAS-29 having never had a green suite run. Both reported honestly in the body; neither had a verdict token for "I could not check." → `qa.md` Lifecycle and `ios-code-reviewer.md` Verdicts both gain **BLOCKED**.
  - **L-031** (tooling, general) — three independent sessions hit hard `ENOSPC` in one window (209MB free of 228GB; 8 concurrent `xcodebuild` processes, 9 booted simulators; the harness's own Bash tool broke). **L-029's own fix caused it** — per-run `-derivedDataPath` trades contention for consumption. Nobody preflighted, because no file asked them to. → `qa.md` 3b and `ios-developer.md`'s build step gain a `df -h /` preflight, a don't-start floor, and a teardown clause.
  - **L-032** (prd-trd, general) — **L-009 marked `failed`.** Three recurrences today (T-032's REJECT F1, T-053's six vacuous §9 rows, T-055's missing z5 row). Root cause of the *fix's* failure: every file L-009 touched (`ios-code-reviewer.md`, `qa.md`, `product.md`) is a **consumer** of §9; `architect.md`, which governs how §9 is authored, was never touched. Patching detectors leaves the production rate untouched. → `architect.md` §9 gains three authoring rules (every normative must/never in §2–§5 gets a row; no pass condition satisfiable over an empty set; rendered requirements checked on rendered output, never `exists`/`label`), with `ios-code-reviewer.md`'s `trd-review` bullet as the backstop. This also answers the call `architect` explicitly left to this agent in its own T-055 entry.
  - **L-033** (process, general) — a claim is not a heartbeat. T-036/PAS-27 and T-037/PAS-28's code-reviews sat `In Progress` from 2026-08-02 with zero artifacts of any kind; round 8 correctly applied L-007 and did **not** re-dispatch them, which is what froze both for ~24h. → `chief-of-staff.md` gains one bullet: brief every dispatched agent to commit a durable claim artifact first, and treat a claimed task with no commit/no worklog entry/no uncommitted file as abandoned rather than in flight.
- **Six past fixes verified** (7+ days, no recurrence, positive evidence today): L-002 (founder-direct stubs — used twice today), L-003 (newest-first worklog — ~30 entries today, all correct), L-005 (full-target test runs), L-006 (PRD status discipline), L-007 (fresh re-read before dispatch), L-010 (flake triage lands in a durable place). L-023's second fix is holding but is only a day old — several sessions today named other sessions' carried entries explicitly rather than sweeping them, exactly as the rule asks; left at `applied`, re-check in a week.
- **Evidence:** all five edited agent files re-`diff`ed against both mirrors after copying — silent on all ten comparisons. Line counts after edit: `qa.md` 37, `architect.md` 57, `ios-developer.md` 75, `ios-code-reviewer.md` 43, `chief-of-staff.md` 223 — every change inside the ~10% size budget, three of them in place with no line added.

### 2026-08-03 — architect — T-055 time-slider TRD §9: the z5 layout rule finally gets a verification row (TRD v4)

- **Did:** `prds/time-slider/TRD.md` → **v4**. One substantive addition — **§9 row 5b**, the check §2.3's z5 layering rule never had. Plus a §9 preamble carrying three standing rules, a matching §10 risk row, and the v4 changelog block. **§2.3's z5 rule itself is unchanged** — it was correct as written and the build deviated from it; this fixes the *gate*, not the *design*.
- **The row, and why it is shaped this way.** Observable is the **rendered frames**, never the padding constant: with the heat modal presented, `HeatModalCard.frame` and `MapNavRow.frame` must not intersect, separated by a fixed gap (≥8pt — an **[ASSUMPTION]** floor, since §2.3 requires the distance be *fixed and non-zero* and names no number; the constant is `ios-developer`'s at build, and if a different fixed distance is chosen the floor moves to it). Asserted **separately for each of the three readout elements** — offset numeral, clock label, "next day" pill — because the card can clear the nav row while a pill drawn at its edge does not. Run **twice**: default text size *and* AX5, since the card is sized-to-content and a default-size gap proves nothing about the AX5 one. Plus `card.frame.maxY <` safe-area bottom, so `bottom: 0` is falsified by name rather than inferred from the gap. Non-empty frames are asserted **first**, with values recorded — two frames that never rendered do not intersect either, which is exactly how this row could pass while the requirement fails. Layer is a **rendered UI test**, explicitly not unit, not source review, not `exists` — all three passed this build. Steps **C2, C5**: C2 wires the nav row in at z7, C5 builds the card, and neither alone can check a relationship it owns half of.
- **One thing I was careful not to claim.** The positive control (the pill's `label` reads `"next day"` in full) **does not detect the occlusion** and the row says so in as many words. `XCUIElement.label` returns the whole string for a fully covered `Text` — that is precisely why F1 survived `qa`. Its only job is to prove the elements rendered at all, so the geometric assertions are claims about something. A row that leaned on the label check would have reproduced the bug it exists to catch.
- **Standing rules — lifted locally, deliberately not workspace-wide.** §9's preamble now states the two rules T-053 added to the `search-quick-filters` TRD (no pass condition satisfiable over an empty set; every negative-existence check needs a positive control) plus a third for this feature's shape (rendered-result rows are run on a rendered app; blocked → **BLOCKED**, reported as unrun). Restated rather than cross-referenced because `qa` reads this table standalone. **Rows 1–7 were not re-audited against them in this pass** and the preamble says so — a partial audit silently presented as a full one is the same class of problem as a vacuous pass condition. Whether these belong in `architect.md` as fleet-wide rules is `retrospective`'s call; I did not make it here.
- **Documentation-only — this blocks nothing.** No code, no schema, no contract, no decision reopened, no build step changed. **No `trd-review` re-run is owed and this does not gate `ios-developer`'s in-flight T-032 rebuild**, whose correctness was already fully specified by §2.3 z5 and PRD v9's req 5 bullet, both predating v4. Same shape as T-053's §9 amendment.
- **Third instance of one failure shape (L-009):** T-046, then T-053/`PAS-43`, now this. All three are a real rule stated somewhere no gate reads.
- **HTML twins regenerated, and one of them is not mine — disclosed rather than flagged.** Ran `prds/md2html.py` (17 files written, 3 changed on disk): `time-slider/TRD.html` (mine, from this v4), `time-slider/time-slider.html` (stale since `product`'s PRD v9 at `73210bc`), `search-quick-filters/search-quick-filters.html` (stale since T-053's amendment, source committed at `9c827af`). Both non-mine files are mechanical renders of **already-committed** markdown, so no other session's in-flight work is swept in. Staged and named in the commit message per `bfd0c7e`'s precedent — `time-slider/TRD.html` is the exact artifact L-026/L-027 are about, and a fourth "flagged for whoever owns it" was not an option.
- **Left behind:** (1) **No Linear issue for T-055** — deliberately not invented, `chief-of-staff` creates it (T-053 labelled itself `PAS-41`, which was a different task, and three references needed correcting after the fact). (2) The 8pt gap floor is an **[ASSUMPTION]**; if `ios-developer` picks a different fixed distance at the rebuild, row 5b's number moves with it — what may not move is that the gap is fixed, positive, and asserted. (3) Row 5b binds this TRD only. The four sibling z5 surfaces (`PassportSurface`, `PlacesListOverlay`, `SearchOverlay`) share the anchoring pattern and are **T-054**'s, named in the new §10 row so nobody reads 5b as covering them. (4) Rows 1–7 unaudited against the three standing rules, above.
- **Git:** `passenger-brain` — `prds/time-slider/TRD.md`, `prds/time-slider/TRD.html`, `prds/time-slider/time-slider.html`, `prds/search-quick-filters/search-quick-filters.html`, `agent-os/BOARD.md` (T-055 → `done`), `agent-os/PROGRESS.md` (this entry). Explicit paths, no `-A`. `design/map-rendering-spec.md` was modified in the shared tree by another session and is **not** staged. No `passenger-code` change. **Not pushed** — Aviran-gated, `CLAUDE.md` rule 9. Hash in the final response.

### 2026-08-03 — product — T-032/PAS-15 acceptance pass: **REJECT → `build`**, and the 11 environment-blocked QA rows re-run live

- **Did:** the `acceptance` step on T-032/PAS-15, arriving from `qa`'s PASS WITH MINORS. Read `passenger-brain/CLAUDE.md`, `BOARD.md`'s T-032 row in full (both tables), this file's recent worklog (`qa`'s T-032 entry, `ios-code-reviewer`'s T-032 code-review, my own T-034/T-035/T-036 pass for method), the PRD (v8) and `TRD.md` v3, and `TEST-PLAN.md`'s 27 cases. Then re-derived every P0 from `passenger-code` source rather than from `qa`'s verdict — the same discipline as the T-033/T-034/T-035 passes — and, because disk headroom had returned, **ran the blocked cases live instead of accepting them on a source read**.
- **Scope gate (Job 0), run on inherited work:** clean. PRD Motivation carries the strategy line verbatim — *"Time slider, now → +12 hours. A place's relevance changes by the hour; the map knows that. Unchanged."* Nothing social, no onboarding, no Phase 2/3 hooks. The gesture change (v2/v3) doesn't need a new citation: the line names the control and its range, not how a finger reaches it.

**Verdict: REJECT → `build`. Two blocking findings, both rendered-only, both invisible to every gate that ran before this one.**

- **F1 (blocking) — the nav row draws on top of the modal's readout, at the DEFAULT text size.** Not an accessibility edge case: `MapNavRow`'s Heat and Search buttons sit on the "next day" flag in `HourReadout`, which renders as "…t day". At +12h/11:00 that matters — "+12h 11:00" without the flag reads as 11:00 *today*, so the readout can state the wrong hour. **This is a straight deviation from the TRD's own §2.3 z-table**, which specifies z5 as *"anchored a fixed distance above the nav row, sized to content — never `bottom: 0`"*. The build ships `HeatModalCard` as `ZStack(alignment: .bottom)` + `.padding(.bottom, 8)`, while `MapScreen.swift` places `MapNavRow` in `.overlay(alignment: .bottom).padding(.bottom, 96)` — so the card grows upward straight through the nav row's band. The z7 row's other half ("never covered") is satisfied; the z5 half is not, and the pair only works together.
- **F2 (blocking) — the readout is not legible at the largest supported Dynamic Type size.** At AX5 the offset label wraps mid-token ("+12" on one line, "h" on the next) and the "next day" pill breaks across two lines inside a capsule that no longer contains it. `grep` for `dynamicTypeSize` across `Passenger/` returns nothing, so AX5 genuinely is "the largest supported size" — there is no cap making this out of scope. Re-check F2 after F1's fix: a correctly anchored card may reflow, but the mid-token wrap is a layout failure independent of the occlusion.
- **Why no earlier gate caught either.** `code-review` reviewed against the TRD end-to-end but never rendered the app. `qa`'s C16 (the AX5 row) was one of the 11 the disk exhaustion blocked. And nothing anywhere covered F1: §2.3's z-table is stated as architecture and **never became a §9 verification row or a TEST-PLAN case**, so no gate had a rendered pass condition to fail. Element-existence assertions cannot see this — `XCUIElement.exists` is `true` for a fully occluded label — and neither can a source read. **Spec defect as much as a build defect (L-009): req 5 gains the missing rendered bullet in the same commit as this verdict**, stated as what the user sees, not as a stored value. PRD → **Draft v9**, status deliberately *not* flipped to Accepted.

**The 11 BLOCKED rows, re-run live — `qa`'s environment blocker is gone and both reviewer asks are now closed.**

- **Environment:** `df -h /` showed 45Gi free, `ps aux` zero concurrent `xcodebuild`, one booted simulator — the wall three sessions hit today is down. Ran on a **clean detached `git worktree` at `passenger-code d537ca5`**, deliberately not the shared working tree, which held T-051/T-052 WIP in `MapScreen.swift`/`EventDetailModal.swift` — testing T-032 against another task's uncommitted edits would not have been evidence about T-032. Dedicated simulator, own `derivedDataPath`.
- **C21 (reviewer's ask #1) — PASS, live.** `EdgeHourZoneInteractionTests` executed 3/3 clean in 64.2s, zero failures, including `testInBandVerticalDragMovesTheHourAndLeavesTheCameraUntouched`: camera byte-identical across two real consecutive in-band drags while `selectedHour` moved. `qa` re-verified this test's *construction*; this pass re-verified its *result*.
- **C26 (reviewer's ask #2) — PASS, live.** No session on record had ever performed the rendered rotation check. Wrote a throwaway probe: rotate to `.landscapeLeft`, perform an in-band vertical edge drag → hour unchanged at `0`; rotate back to portrait, perform the **identical** drag → hour moves. The negative control is the whole point — without it, "nothing happened in landscape" is equally consistent with a drag that simply missed.
- **C5 — PASS, live.** `HeatRepaintPerformanceTests` measured `HourRepaint` at **average 0.004s**, values `[0.0043, 0.0056, 0.0039, 0.0036, 0.0025]`, against req 2's 400ms budget. Two orders of magnitude of headroom, as the "no fetch on an hour change" architecture predicted.
- **C6 — PASS, closed by probe.** The button-path camera check `qa` logged as a minor gap: swept the slider 0→12, camera dump identical before/mid/after. **The gap is now a missing test, not a missing answer** — the shipped suite still has no such case.
- **C22–C25 — resolved by construction, not blocked.** The four rows split on sheet *detent*, and there is no detent-sensitive code path to split on: `EdgeAvailability.liveEdges` takes no detent argument, and `edgeLayer(for:)` removes `EdgeHourZone` from the hierarchy whenever `isAnySheetPresented`. `.medium` and `.large` cannot differ. `testEdgeZoneIsInertWhileAHoodSheetIsPresented` also ran clean live.
- **C13 — PASS (structural).** Both paths draw the "now" mark as `diamond.fill` against hairline `Rectangle` (slider) / 3pt `Circle` (edge track) stops — shape-distinct, so greyscale is irrelevant.
- **400/400 unit tests green** (71 suites, Swift Testing) on the same worktree.
- **Still open, non-blocking:** C14 (no live VoiceOver session — unchanged from `qa`'s position); C20 — confirmed the intended control case was never written, `EdgeHourZoneInteractionTests.mapCenterX` is declared with a comment naming the "pan starts outside the band" case and is referenced by no test in the file.
- **One thing I checked and did NOT count as a finding.** Req 6's button-path bullet says the slider's frame is "≥44pt **on the drag axis**". Measured live: the drawn/hit-testable frame is 354×**31pt**, and a horizontal drag 20pt above its centre — inside the 44pt band `.frame(minHeight: 44)` claims, outside the drawn control — does not move the hour, while the identical drag through the centre moves it 0→12. So the layout container is 44pt and the *touch target* is 31pt. **The bullet still passes as written**, because Q5 (v4) confirmed the button control stays horizontal, making the drag axis the 354pt one. The bullet measures the axis that cannot fail and leaves the acquisition axis unspecified — but 31pt is Apple's own stock `Slider` height, and holding it to 44pt means abandoning the native control, which req 6's VoiceOver bullet and req 6's platform-drawn rail exemption both depend on. That is a scope call (the same shape as Q7, which Aviran ratified), not a `product` call, and not something to smuggle in under a REJECT. **Not raised as a finding, not fixed silently — flagged here so it is on record**, and worth Aviran's ruling if he wants it, separately from this rejection.
- **Cross-task finding, filed rather than folded in:** all four z5 surfaces share the anchoring pattern F1 exposes — `PassportSurface`, `PlacesListOverlay` (both `.padding(.bottom, 8)`) and `SearchOverlay` (`.padding(.bottom, 4)`) all sit under the same 96pt-high floating nav row. Confirmed by source read, **not** by rendering them. Filed as **T-054** with `ios-developer` as owner, per `CLAUDE.md` rule 5 — a defect I find but don't own gets a board row, not a worklog line. Deliberately not folded into T-032's fix: those are three other PRDs' surfaces, two of them already accepted.
- **What `ios-developer` needs to fix, numbered:** (1) anchor `HeatModalCard` a fixed distance above `MapNavRow` per TRD §2.3 z5, so the two never share pixels — not by hiding the nav row, which z7 forbids; (2) make the readout row survive AX5 without wrapping mid-token or breaking the pill. `architect` owes §9 a verification row for the z5 layout rule, which is what let this through.
- **Left behind:** (1) T-054, above. (2) Two cheap tests the shipped suite lacks and this pass proved are writable — a rotation check (C26) and a button-path camera check (C6); probes were throwaway and are not committed to `passenger-code`. (3) C14's live VoiceOver run and C20's control case, both still open and both non-blocking. (4) The 44pt/31pt touch-target question above, for Aviran if he wants it. (5) T-050 is still blocked on this task's C5 — that measurement now exists (4ms), so T-050's re-verification half is unblocked the moment T-032 lands.
- **Git:** `passenger-brain` — `prds/time-slider/time-slider.md` (v9: status line, req 5 bullet, Decisions log row), `prds/time-slider/TEST-PLAN.md` (acceptance addendum; `qa`'s own rows left as written), `agent-os/BOARD.md` (T-032 both rows → `build`/`ios-developer`, new T-054 row), `agent-os/PROGRESS.md` (this entry). Explicit paths, no `-A`. No `passenger-code` change — an acceptance verdict writes no app code, and the throwaway probes and the scratch worktree were removed. **Not pushed** — Aviran-gated, `CLAUDE.md` rule 9. Hash in the final response.

### 2026-08-03 — qa — PAS-41 CI workflow QA pass (`passenger-code 2e8dc53`): **PASS**

- **Did:** bug-ticket-style QA on the GitHub Actions build gate `ios-developer` added and `ios-code-reviewer` already APPROVEd (both entries immediately below). Read `BOARD.md` in full and this file's recent worklog (both entries) first, per protocol. No PRD/TRD/TEST-PLAN for this task — it's infra, matches the reviewer's own N/A calls. Two things independently verified rather than trusted from either prior entry: the build reproduces, and the YAML is well-formed.
- **Build — independently reproduced, not reused from `ios-developer`'s or the reviewer's own local runs.** Ran the exact CI command on a dedicated, previously-unused `-derivedDataPath` in scratch (not a shared/default one): `xcodebuild build -project Passenger.xcodeproj -scheme Passenger -destination 'generic/platform=iOS Simulator' CODE_SIGNING_ALLOWED=NO` → **BUILD SUCCEEDED**, `grep -c "error:"` on the full log → `0`. Checked `ps aux`/`xcrun simctl list devices` first per L-029 — three other sessions had concurrent `xcodebuild` processes and booted simulators (`T032-Accept`, `T051-Verify-*`) at the time, but `generic/platform=iOS Simulator` is a device-less destination (no simulator boot required), so this run had no UDID-contention exposure regardless; disk was healthy for this run (`df -h /`: 37Gi avail, 24% — the ENOSPC condition other sessions hit earlier today had cleared).
- **YAML well-formedness — checked with a generic parser, not GitHub's own.** `ruby -ryaml` (pyyaml wasn't installed, same substitute `ios-developer` used) parses the file cleanly into the expected structure: `name`, `on: {push: {branches: [main]}, pull_request: {branches: [main]}}`, one job (`build`, `runs-on: macos-15`) with three steps (Checkout, Select latest stable Xcode, Build). One thing worth naming, not a defect: a generic YAML 1.1 loader resolves the bare `on:` key to the boolean `true` rather than the string `"on"` (the well-known YAML "Norway problem") — cosmetic in the parsed dict, and not a GitHub Actions bug, since GitHub's own workflow schema special-cases `on:` as the trigger key and every workflow file in existence uses it unquoted this way. No `actionlint` available in this environment to cross-check against GitHub's actual schema; the generic-parser pass plus direct visual inspection of triggers/runner/steps against the ticket's stated spec is what this verdict rests on.
- **Independently re-confirmed the "nothing pushed yet" claim** rather than taking the two build entries' word for it: `git fetch origin main` + `git log origin/main..HEAD` shows `2e8dc53` and its parent `d537ca5` both ahead of `origin/main`; `git branch -r --contains 2e8dc53` returns nothing. Confirms the DoD item "a real push/PR triggers it and completes successfully" is still open — expected, not a QA failure, per the dispatch brief.
- **Verdict: PASS.** No blocking findings, nothing new to add to the reviewer's non-blocking minors list (timeout-minutes, concurrency group, DerivedData caching — not re-litigated here, still open for whoever next touches this file). This is the last internal gate before `chief-of-staff` reports to Aviran; the live-Actions-run DoD item stays open until he pushes.

### 2026-08-03 — ios-code-reviewer — PAS-41 CI workflow review (`passenger-code 2e8dc53`): **APPROVE**

- **Did:** first review of `.github/workflows/ios-build.yml` (new file, 33 lines), the build-only GitHub Actions gate `ios-developer` added at `passenger-code 2e8dc53`. Read `BOARD.md` in full and this file's recent worklog first, per protocol — found no `BOARD.md` row for PAS-41 (this task isn't tracked there, only in the worklog and Linear), so nothing to move on that board; confirmed via `grep -n "PAS-41\|CI\|ios-build" agent-os/BOARD.md` (zero hits). No PRD/TRD exists for this task (it's infra, not a feature) — TRD-conformance pass N/A. No UI/HIG/Liquid Glass surface touched — those passes N/A. `/vibe-security` pass: clean, no secrets/keys/tokens in the file, no auth/payment/location code touched.
- **Verified against the live project rather than trusting the commit message:** `xcodebuild -list -project Passenger.xcodeproj` confirms `Passenger` is the project's one and only scheme, matching the workflow's `-scheme Passenger`. `grep -c XCRemoteSwiftPackageReference Passenger.xcodeproj/project.pbxproj` → `0` — no remote SPM packages, so the build needs no network dependency resolution beyond `actions/checkout@v4`, removing a whole class of CI flakiness. `SupabaseConfig.plist` is gitignored and referenced only at runtime in `AppConfig.swift`/`BuildPhase.swift` (`grep -rn SupabaseConfig Passenger/`) — its absence in a fresh CI checkout cannot break `xcodebuild build`, since the workflow only compiles, never runs the app.
- **`xcode-version: latest-stable` vs. pinning (the ios-developer's flagged [ASSUMPTION]) — checked, and it's the right call, not just a defensible one.** `project.pbxproj` shows `IPHONEOS_DEPLOYMENT_TARGET = 26.0`, `SWIFT_VERSION = 6.0`, `SWIFT_STRICT_CONCURRENCY = complete` on both build configs — this project targets iOS 26 exclusively, no backward-compat path (matches the fleet's standing "no `if #available`" rule elsewhere). Pinning to an arbitrary specific Xcode number risks landing on one that predates the iOS 26 SDK and hard-failing every run for a reason unrelated to the code being tested; `latest-stable` is the only choice that tracks the SDK the project actually requires. The one residual, unverifiable-from-here risk: whether the `macos-15` runner's current `latest-stable` Xcode already ships the iOS 26 SDK — that's the same "first real push is the actual test" gap `ios-developer` already disclosed in `ONBOARDING.md`, not a defect in this YAML.
- **Build command and destination:** `xcodebuild build -project Passenger.xcodeproj -scheme Passenger -destination 'generic/platform=iOS Simulator' CODE_SIGNING_ALLOWED=NO` is correct for a build-only gate. `generic/platform=iOS Simulator` avoids depending on any specific simulator runtime/device being preinstalled on the runner image — the right choice given the job never launches or tests the app. `CODE_SIGNING_ALLOWED=NO` is required and correctly scoped, since there's no Apple Developer account yet (`ACCOUNTS-AND-COSTS.md`). Trigger scope (`push`/`pull_request` to `main` only) matches the ticket's stated goal of a green build gate, nothing more.
- **Non-blocking minors:** no `timeout-minutes` on the job (GitHub's default is a 6-hour ceiling on hosted runners — cheap insurance against a hung build silently burning Actions minutes); no `concurrency` group to auto-cancel a superseded run on rapid successive pushes; no DerivedData/build caching (low-value here specifically, since there's no SPM network fetch to cache around, but would still speed up repeat builds later). None of these block a first, minimal, "build gate only" gate — worth picking up if/when this workflow grows (e.g. once tests get folded in).
- **Noted, not re-litigated:** the `architect` entry immediately above this one (T-053/`PAS-41` search-quick-filters TRD) already flags that its own Linear ID and this task's Linear ID collide — one of the two is mislabelled. Already surfaced there as `chief-of-staff`'s to resolve in Linear; not re-flagged as a new finding here, just cross-referenced so it isn't lost between the two entries.
- **Verdict: APPROVE.** No blocking findings, no minors that need a fix before this ships. Small, correct, low-risk mechanical change — reviewed to that bar, not padded with invented risk. Moving to `qa`, briefed that there is nothing to behaviorally test here beyond watching the first real Actions run go green once Aviran pushes — the actual open item is the same one `ios-developer` already named: no live GitHub Actions run has been observed yet from any session.
- **Git:** `passenger-brain` — `agent-os/PROGRESS.md` (this entry). Explicit path. No `passenger-code` changes (review only). Not pushed — Aviran-gated, `CLAUDE.md` rule 9. Hash in the final response.

### 2026-08-03 — ios-code-reviewer — T-038/PAS-29 fix-pass code-review (fresh, full): **APPROVE WITH MINORS**

- **Did:** a fresh, full code-review of `passenger-code d537ca5` against `prds/search-quick-filters/TRD.md` and its intersection with `prds/time-slider/TRD.md`/`prds/live-events-overlay/TRD.md` (`HoodLayer`, `MapScreen`) — required because the prior verdict at this gate was a blocking REJECT (T-035/T-036 convention). Not diff-scoped: read `HoodLayer.swift`, `HeatPalette.swift`, `SearchDim.swift`, `CategoryChipRow.swift`, `MapScreen.swift`, and `Place.swift`/`Hood.swift` in their current state, not just the commit's hunks.
- **F1a (heat-fill dim) — independently traced, genuinely fixed.** `HoodLayer.fillColor` (now `internal`, not `private`, so `HoodLayerFillDimTests` can read it directly) is `HeatPalette.hue.opacity(HeatPalette.fillOpacity(for: band, dimmedBy: dimOpacity))`, and `fillOpacity(for:dimmedBy:)` is `opacity(for: band) * dimOpacity` — a pure numeric composition, not a doc-comment claim. `dimOpacity` is `isDimmed ? 0.25 : 1`, the same value the stroke/annotation already used. Traced the full call path, not the commit message. `HeatPaletteTests`/`HoodLayerFillDimTests` (both new) assert the *resolved* `UIColor` alpha is weaker when dimmed, across every band and zoom tier, plus a same-fill-when-undimmed regression guard and a stays-`.clear`-either-way case for no band — this is exactly the "rendered result, not the passed value" standard L-009 asked for, not a re-run of the same shallow assertion that missed it the first time.
- **F1b (place-only match at cold-open zoom) — independently traced, genuinely fixed, and it's the alternate mechanism product's own REJECT text named as "the obvious candidate."** `MapScreen.swift`'s `HoodLayer` `ForEach` (around line 224) is not gated by `showsNames`/zoom tier at all — only the `PlaceLayer` `ForEach` a few lines below is (`if showsNames`, span < 0.06). `SearchDim.emphasis` now adds `place.hoodID` to the `hoods` set for every place match (`Passenger/Search/SearchDim.swift:37`), so `MapScreen.isHoodDimmed` returns `false` for that Hood regardless of pin visibility. Net effect at the shipped cold-open camera (`telAvivCityWide`, span 0.14, `.cityWide`): the matched place's own Hood renders its heat fill at full strength while the other 23 Hoods dim to 0.25 — a real, visible, zoom-independent change. `place.hoodID: String` / `Hood.id: String` types line up (confirmed by reading both structs); this compiles, not just typechecks in my head. `MapScreen.swift` itself is untouched by this commit, which is correct: the fix works around `PlaceLayer`'s zoom gate rather than removing it, matching product's own stated fix direction rather than a novel deviation from the TRD.
- **F2 (chip truncation) — genuinely fixed, mirrors precedent exactly.** `CategoryChip`'s `Text(category.displayName)` now carries `.fixedSize(horizontal: false, vertical: true)`, byte-identical to the modifier `SearchResultRow.swift` already carries on its two text runs (an already-shipped, already-reviewed pattern, not an invented one). **One non-blocking gap:** `SearchRowGrowthGuardTests` (pre-existing, not touched by this commit) asserts `.lineLimit(` absence across all search files and asserts `SearchResultRow`'s own `Text`-run-count-equals-`.fixedSize`-count, but nothing equivalent exists for `CategoryChipRow` — so a future edit to `CategoryChip` could silently drop `.fixedSize` again with no test catching it. Cheap fix, not blocking: extend the existing guard's per-file loop to also count `Text(`/`.fixedSize` pairs in `CategoryChipRow.swift`.
- **P0 spot-check (not full re-derivation, per dispatch brief — 7 of 8 already passed twice):** the diff's surface is narrow (a fill-color computation, a `Set` insertion, one Text modifier) and touches no routing, location-state, session-persistence, or accessibility-label code; `CategoryChip`'s existing `.frame(minHeight: 44)`/`.accessibilityLabel` are unchanged by this commit. No regression risk found on read.
- **`/vibe-security` pass:** clean. No new network calls, no new persistence, no logging, no secrets — the whole diff is pure rendering math (`Double`/`Color` composition) and a `Set<Hood.ID>` insertion from data already on-device.
- **Build:** independently run (not trusted from the commit message) — `xcodebuild -project Passenger.xcodeproj -scheme Passenger -destination 'platform=iOS Simulator,name=iPhone 17e' build` → **BUILD SUCCEEDED**, no errors, no warnings in the touched files.
- **Test — could not be completed, environment-caused, disclosed rather than papered over.** The build machine's root volume was at 99%+ capacity this session (`df -h /`: 266Mi free at first check, dropped to single-digit Mi and then a hard `ENOSPC` mid-session — my own Bash tool started failing on `echo hi`). A full `xcodebuild test` failed with `IDETesting: ... fileSystemFailure(reason: "FileBackedCASDatabase: rename failed: No space left on device")` and `CASTreeDataStructure/Importer.swift: Fatal error: ... mkstemp: No space left on device` — not a single real `XCTAssert...failed` line anywhere, and explicitly **not** the L-029 "Restarting after unexpected exit, crash, or test timeout" pattern; this is disk exhaustion, not simulator-UDID contention. A scoped retry (`-only-testing` on just the three new/changed test files) also failed to even complete for the same reason. Did not attempt to free space by deleting shared `DerivedData` — other sessions were concurrently building (multiple booted simulators observed, consistent with the documented L-029 contention pattern) and unilaterally clearing shared build state risked breaking a concurrent session's in-flight work, which is outside this review's scope to risk. **Confidence the tests would pass regardless:** traced by hand — `HeatPaletteTests`/`HoodLayerFillDimTests` assert `dimmedAlpha < undimmedAlpha`, which holds unconditionally since `opacity(for: band) > 0` for every band and `dimOpacity` is either `0.25` or `1`; `SearchDimTests`' new cases are plain `Set`/dictionary equality checks against code already read in full above. High confidence, not proof — flagged plainly as unexecuted rather than reported as green.
- **Verdict: APPROVE WITH MINORS.** No blocking findings — F1a, F1b, and F2 are all genuinely fixed, independently traced to the rendered/resolved level per L-009's standard, not re-trusted from the commit message. Two non-blocking minors (both above): the `CategoryChipRow` structural-guard gap, and the unexecuted full test run (environment-caused, not a code defect). Moving T-038 to `qa` — flagging for `qa` that this session never got a real green full-suite run, so their pass should be the first one, and that F1a/F1b are best verified live at the app's actual cold-open camera (search "suzan" or similar place-only query, confirm the matched place's Hood visibly holds its heat fill while surrounding Hoods dim) rather than from unit-test evidence alone.
- **Left behind:** (1) the disk-exhaustion infra problem is worth a founder-visible flag — it blocked a real test run and will block the next session's too if unresolved; not something this review can fix (shared machine, concurrent sessions, out of scope to `rm` around). (2) `CategoryChipRow`'s missing structural test-lock (above) — cheap, someone should fold it into `SearchRowGrowthGuardTests` next time that file is touched. (3) `architect`'s still-owed TRD §9 row 4(a)/row 8(c) amendment (T-053, unowned per `BOARD.md`) — untouched by this review, not this agent's to write.
- **Git:** `passenger-brain` — `agent-os/BOARD.md` (T-038 row), `agent-os/PROGRESS.md` (this entry). Explicit paths. No `passenger-code` changes — review only, no fixes applied. Not pushed — Aviran-gated, `CLAUDE.md` rule 9. Hash in the final response.

Full `xcodebuild test` — 9 UI-test "failures" surfaced (`ColdOpenPerformanceTests`, both `EdgeHourZoneInteractionTests`, `HeatButtonInteractionTests.testTappingHeatButtonAgainDismissesTheModal`, `HeatRepaintPerformanceTests`, `LocalQAToastInteractionTests`, `ProfileButtonInteractionTests`, two `SearchAccessibilityTests`), spanning five unrelated tasks' test files. No location data logged/persisted — the debug camera dump is UI-test-only and never active in a real launch. `selectedHour` stays in-memory per §3.1, confirmed by grep (no `UserDefaults`/`AppStorage`/file write of the hour anywhere in the diff).
